**Workbook fit**

| Session | Fit | Where it lands | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Making It Rain | primary | Your Marketing Strategy | This is a channel decision with unusually concrete mechanics — which ZIPs, which filters, what the envelope looks like, how the response arrives. That makes the strategy conversation specific instead of abstract. The owner still decides spend, ZIP selection and what a responder is worth. |
| The Sales Sequence | supporting_direct | Accountability | A mail drop puts inbound calls on a producer's line on a known date, arriving with the prospect's own home and a quoted premium already in front of them. That is a defined starting point for a call, which is what this session asks you to write. It does not write the call or score it. |
| The Operating System | conditional | Your Agency Bible | A four-step process with a named onboarding form, a quote matrix and a job upload path means the marketing handoff is a documented, repeatable procedure rather than a phone call to the owner. Conditional because that procedure is the vendor's, and only the handoff edge of it lands inside your agency. |
| Growth Through Service | conditional | Synchronicity | Variable data printing and list targeting also work on a book you already own — renewal and cross-sell touches to existing households, not just prospecting. Conditional because nothing published describes a retention program; that use is an owner's decision to redirect the tool. |
| Commitment to Training | not_recommended | — | No training product, curriculum, workshop or certification is published. A newsletter of marketing inspiration is not a training system for producers or service staff. |
| The Body Session | not_recommended | — | Company research is deliberately excluded from the personal workbooks at runtime. |
| The Being Session | not_recommended | — | Company research is deliberately excluded from the personal workbooks at runtime. |
| The Balance Session | not_recommended | — | Company research is deliberately excluded from the personal workbooks at runtime. |
| My 2027 Map | reference_only | — | The Map compiles decisions already made in the other sessions. Company research is not loaded here and should not introduce anything new. |

**Room exercises and measures**

- _Making It Rain_ exercise: Name the three ZIPs you are actually competitive in, and the premium number you would put on a mock quote there.
- _Making It Rain_ measure: Pieces mailed, calls received and quoted households per drop, per ZIP.
- _The Sales Sequence_ exercise: Write the first thirty seconds of the call for a homeowner holding a letter with their own house on it.
- _The Sales Sequence_ measure: Mail-sourced calls answered live, quoted and bound, by producer, per drop.
- _The Operating System_ exercise: Write down who in your office fills out the quote matrix and approves the proof, and what happens when they are out.
- _The Operating System_ measure: Proof approvals returned within the agreed window, out of total drops.
- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: Decide the one moment in a client's year that deserves a physical piece of mail, and what it says.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Existing-household touches mailed per month, and cross-sold policies attributed to them.

**On these resource pages**

- **Making It Rain Resources** (/resources/making-it-rain)
  - Helps with: Direct mail campaigns built for insurance agents, including targeting, creative and fulfillment.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves reaching households in your territory through mail rather than digital channels.

**Open questions**

- All pricing. No cost per piece, minimum drop size, setup fee or design fee is published; everything runs through a discovery call.
- Minimum campaign volume and required cadence. Nothing published on how many pieces a drop must be or how often drops must repeat.
- ZIP or territory exclusivity. The site says responses are exclusive to the agent but never says whether a ZIP is locked to one agent, which is the material question for a mailer.
- What the agency has to supply: agent photo, office phone number, logo, carrier-approved copy, the mock quote premiums and the approval turnaround are all implied but never specified.
- Where the mailing list comes from — data vendor, property records or a list the agent provides — and whether the agent may keep or re-use it.
- How responses are delivered and tracked. QR and call routing are described, but not what reporting the agent receives afterwards, or whether calls are recorded or scored.
- Carrier and captive-brand compliance review for mail copy and quoted premiums.
- Print turnaround and in-home date windows.
- What Custom Print and Design Services cost or include as standalone lines.
- The volume counters on every page render as zero, so the company's own delivered-pieces figure is not actually published.

**Activation notes**

- runtimeSurface: Partner-seat business workbooks only (s1, s2, s4, s5, s7)
- identityStatus: awaiting_account_and_organization
- note: Research is not loaded by runtime until bound. Approve the record, bind the exact existing organization, then separately authorize the Firebase Functions release. Book mapping is editorial analysis, not partner-approved placement.

### 14. NW Preferred Federal Credit Union

- **Tier:** Bronze
- **Website:** https://nwpreferredfcu.com
- **Logo:** `/assets/sponsors/nw-preferred.png`
- **Resource-page id:** `nw-preferred`
- **Category:** Federal credit union lending to insurance agents: contract-value and TPP lending, agency acquisition, commercial real estate and business banking
- **Also known as:** NW Preferred, NW Preferred FCU, Northwest Preferred Federal Credit Union
- **Research version:** 2026-09-09.1, reviewed 2026-09-09

**Podcast interview**

- Guest: Fred Jordan
- Vimeo id: 1220555093
- Contact: Call Fred → tel:+15034319868
- Shown as: 503-431-9868
- Note: Fred shared his phone number as the best way to connect with NW Preferred FCU.

**What we know (25 researched facts)**

- **entity** — NW Preferred is a federal credit union, not a bank, and states that serving people working in the insurance industry across the U.S. is the reason it exists. Its published routing number is 323076575 and its published lender identifier is NMLS #648677. _(public_company_claim)_
- **origin** — The credit union states it was chartered on May 9, 1940 in Portland, Oregon to serve the agents and employees of Farmers Insurance Group, and later expanded its service area to all of Oregon and Washington. _(public_company_claim)_
- **tip_charter** — The company states that in 2003 the National Credit Union Administration awarded it the nation's first Trade, Industry, Profession (TIP) charter, allowing it to serve licensed insurance professionals and their employees throughout the Pacific Northwest. _(public_company_claim)_
- **nationwide** — The company states that in 2016 it received approval to serve the licensed insurance industry nationwide, including all territories and possessions of the United States. It also serves anyone who lives, works or worships in the greater North Santiam communities of Oregon. _(public_company_claim)_
- **eligibility** — Eligibility is occupational rather than geographic for agents: a licensed insurance professional anywhere in the U.S. can be a member. The site sorts visitors into four paths on entry - Farmers agent or district manager, Allstate agent, other agent, and not an agent. _(public_company_claim)_
- **collateral_basis** — The distinguishing capability is lending against the agent's carrier contract rather than against hard collateral: available TPP for Allstate agents and available Contract Value including bonus for Farmers agents. That is what lets an agency be financed without real estate or outside security. _(public_company_claim)_
- **tpp_loan** — The TPP Loan is described as offered exclusively to Allstate agents. Published terms: borrow up to 75 percent of available TPP, terms up to 15 years, no application or annual fees, and no pre-payment penalties. The page carries an asterisked disclosure on each figure. _(public_company_claim)_
- **tpp_uses** — Published TPP Loan uses: improve the office, manage cash flow, acquire other books of business, hire staff, renovate the office, and consolidate business debt. The page states it can cover pretty much any business expense. _(public_company_claim)_
- **internal_buy** — The Allstate Internal Buy Loan is for an established Allstate agent purchasing an existing Allstate agency. Published structure: borrow up to 80 percent of your own available TPP plus up to 80 percent of the available TPP of the book being purchased, terms up to 15 years, plus up to an additional $75,000 maximum as a separate acquisition loan. _(public_company_claim)_
- **contract_value_loan** — The Farmers Contract Value Loan lets an agent borrow up to 75 percent of available Contract Value including bonus, with terms up to 15 years and lines of credit available up to $300,000. Automatic folio deduction is available for Contract Value Secured Loans. _(public_company_claim)_
- **farmers_acquisition** — The Farmers Internal Acquisition Loan mirrors the Allstate structure: up to 80 percent of your available Contract Value including bonus plus up to 80 percent of the available Contract Value of the book being purchased, terms up to 15 years, no pre-payment penalties, and qualified applicants may be eligible for up to an additional $75,000 as a separate acquisition loan. _(public_company_claim)_
- **acquisition_pattern** — Both acquisition programs advance against two books at once - the buyer's own contract value and the seller's - which is the structural reason an agent can buy a book larger than their own borrowing base would otherwise support. The advance rate rises from 75 percent on a growth loan to 80 percent on an acquisition. _(public_company_claim)_
- **not_personal_credit** — The company states that business loans for Farmers agents are not reported on personal credit, so agency borrowing does not sit on the owner's personal credit report. _(public_company_claim)_
- **no_audits** — Two published terms distinguish it from other agency lenders: no pre-payment penalties, and no monthly or quarterly audits of the borrower. _(public_company_claim)_
- **farmers_lineup** — The Farmers product lineup is published as Contract Value Loan, Contract Value Loan for District Managers, Acquisition Loan, Commercial Real Estate Loan, Prime Agent Loan and Agency Staffing Loan - so district managers and staffing are financed as named products, not exceptions. _(public_company_claim)_
- **allstate_lineup** — The Allstate lineup is published as TPP Loan, Purchase Another Allstate Agency (Internal Buy Loan), Commercial Real Estate Loan and Business Line of Credit, alongside a Money Market Account and a Health Savings Account for employee benefits. _(public_company_claim)_
- **real_estate** — Commercial real estate lending is positioned as be your own landlord - moving an agency from leasing its office to owning it - and is published for both the Allstate and Farmers channels. _(public_company_claim)_
- **business_products** — The business menu links Business Checking, Agency Trust Checking, Health Savings Account, Commercial Real Estate Loans, Business Visa Credit Card, Office Improvement Loan, Business Unsecured Loan and Business Line of Credit. Agency Trust Checking is a purpose-built account for agency premium trust handling. _(public_company_claim)_
- **process** — The published application process is three steps - apply online in less than 10 minutes, review, approval - with a loan payment calculator on the product pages and a separate link to check application status. _(public_company_claim)_
- **not_for_profit** — The company positions its underwriting posture on being a member-owned not-for-profit whose stated only goal is to help members succeed, and says it will not apply one size fits all - each loan gets a rate and term fitted to the borrower. _(public_company_claim)_
- **deposit_side** — On the deposit side the site publishes share certificates with terms from 6 to 72 months, free checking that earns dividends on balances over $500 with no minimum balance or monthly fees, and tiered money market and savings rates. Products are described as shaped around commission-based income. _(public_company_claim)_
- **digital** — Digital services published include online and mobile banking with balances, transfers, bill pay and alerts, and Zelle inside the mobile app. _(public_company_claim)_
- **preferred_lender_claim** — The Farmers page calls the credit union a Preferred Lender for Farmers Insurance agents. That designation implies a carrier relationship and was not confirmed with Farmers or with the company. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **speed_claims** — The site promises agents will hear back within one business day, guaranteed, and claims better rates and terms than almost anywhere else on the strength of over 80 years lending to agents. These are undated service and comparative claims with no published benchmark. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **testimonials** — Named member testimonials on the product pages cite specific outcomes, including refinancing a TPP loan and saving $30,000 in interest, approval on an agency purchase in three business days, and closing in days rather than weeks. These are member statements published by the lender and were not verified. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_

**Sources**

- About us - NW Preferred Federal Credit Union — https://nwpreferredfcu.com/about/about-us/ (seen 2026-09-09)

**Workbook fit**

| Session | Fit | Where it lands | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The Operating System | primary | An Office Built on Authenticity | Perpetuation is an operating-system question before it is a money question: what the agency is worth, what it can borrow against, who owns the office, and where premium trust money sits. Lending against contract value rather than hard collateral is what makes those decisions financeable. The lender supplies the capital; the succession plan and the written standard are still the owner's to author. |
| The Sales Sequence | conditional | Recruit Recruit Recruit | An Agency Staffing Loan is a named product and hiring staff is a published use of the TPP and Contract Value loans, so headcount an owner cannot fund from cash flow can be financed against the book. Conditional because financing a seat is not the same as defining it, filling it or holding it accountable. |
| Making It Rain | conditional | Your Marketing Strategy | Business lines of credit up to $300,000 and cash-flow financing are published for exactly the uneven spend a marketing program creates. Conditional because the credit union funds the spend and takes no position on the channel or the return. |
| Growth Through Service | conditional | Elite Onboarding Experience | An acquisition loan hands an agency a book of strangers overnight, and that is a service problem the day after closing. Conditional in the narrow sense that the financing decision sets the onboarding load; the lender delivers no service capability whatsoever and should not be presented as helping with retention. |
| Commitment to Training | not_recommended | — | The published offering is lending, deposits and business banking. No curriculum, certification, scoring or coaching exists anywhere on the pages observed. |
| The Body Session | not_recommended | — | Company research is deliberately excluded from the personal workbooks at runtime. |
| The Being Session | not_recommended | — | Company research is deliberately excluded from the personal workbooks at runtime. |
| The Balance Session | not_recommended | — | Company research is deliberately excluded from the personal workbooks at runtime. |
| My 2027 Map | reference_only | — | The Map compiles decisions already made in the other sessions. Company research is not loaded here and should not introduce a financing decision that was not made earlier. |

**Room exercises and measures**

- _The Operating System_ exercise: Write your available contract value, then write the number you would need to buy the book you actually want. Name the gap out loud.
- _The Operating System_ measure: Available contract value against the 75 percent growth and 80 percent acquisition advance rates, and annual debt service as a share of commission.
- _The Sales Sequence_ exercise: Price the producer seat you keep deferring, then decide whether you are short on capital or short on a written seat definition.
- _The Sales Sequence_ measure: Seats financed versus seats hitting the written daily activity floor by day 90.
- _Making It Rain_ exercise: Name the channel you would fund on a line rather than out of cash, and write what it must return before the next draw.
- _Making It Rain_ measure: Line draws against premium written from the funded channel in the same period.
- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: Before you finance a book, write the first 30 days of contact the acquired households will actually receive, and who runs it.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Retention of the acquired book at 12 months against the retention assumed in the loan model.

**On these resource pages**

- **Funding the Build** (/resources/funding-the-build)
  - Helps with: Credit union banking and lending services for members and their businesses.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves financing options for the agency or for the owner behind it.

**Open questions**

- Interest rates and fees. Every product page shows Rates as low as followed by an asterisk with the number behind a View Rates link, and a separate rates page exists but was not captured here because published rates change. Confirm current rates directly.
- The asterisked disclosures behind every advance rate and term figure. The 75 percent, 80 percent, 15-year and $75,000 numbers all carry footnotes that were not read.
- What TPP stands for and exactly how available TPP and available Contract Value including bonus are calculated. The site uses the carrier terms without defining them.
- Credit union membership requirements, share deposit, and whether membership must exist before a loan application.
- Whether an agent outside Allstate and Farmers gets an equivalent acquisition program. The Other Agent path publishes an Agency Business Loan, an Office Improvement Loan and commercial real estate, but no contract-value or acquisition structure was observed for that segment.
- Underwriting criteria: minimum book size, minimum time in the seat, credit score floor, debt-service coverage, or personal guarantee.
- Maximum loan size on an acquisition beyond the stated advance rates and the $75,000 supplemental piece.
- Whether the not-reported-on-personal-credit treatment applies to Allstate borrowers as well. It is published only on the Farmers pages.
- Any portfolio size, loan volume, approval rate or member-count figure. None is published and none was asserted.
- NW Preferred's own org chart, capacity, assigned Formula contact and 2027 targets.

**Activation notes**

- runtimeSurface: Partner-seat business workbooks only (s1, s2, s4, s5, s7)
- identityStatus: no_representative_checked
- disclosure: Research compiled from eight public pages on nwpreferredfcu.com observed on 2026-09-09. No contact with the credit union was made and no account was verified.
- note: Approve the record and bind an existing organization before any runtime load. Book mapping is editorial analysis, not partner-approved placement. Advance rates and terms carry asterisked disclosures that were not read; re-verify before the expiry date.

### 15. Performology

- **Tier:** Bronze
- **Website:** https://performology.com
- **Logo:** `/assets/sponsors/performology.svg`
- **Resource-page id:** `performology`
- **Category:** Insurance agency employee performance management, goal alignment and incentive compensation software
- **Also known as:** Performology Performance Management System, app.performology.com
- **Research version:** 2026-09-09.1, reviewed 2026-09-09

**What we know (20 researched facts)**

- **category** — Performology describes itself as a complete business and employee performance management system for insurance agency owners. _(public_company_claim)_
- **thesis** — The stated premise is that in a business facing constantly changing rates, guidelines and objectives, an insurance agency's success ultimately depends on one factor: the ability to maximise employee performance. _(public_company_claim)_
- **goal_alignment** — The core mechanic is goal alignment: creating SMART goals for each staff member that mirror the agency's parent company goals, so corporate, the agency and all staff are aligned. This assumes a captive or franchise model with a parent carrier setting objectives. _(public_company_claim)_
- **incentive_comp** — The system pairs aligned goals with incentive compensation, described as the way to inspire and motivate maximum performance so that success becomes systemic across the organisation. _(public_company_claim)_
- **three_moves** — The site frames how top sales organisations build teams in three moves: get everyone committed and on the same page, broadcast what is expected and where we are, and incentivise to accelerate results. _(public_company_claim)_
- **integration** — Performology states it has an integration platform intended to let an agency manage the business alongside its existing lead management, CRM and reporting tools. No named integration partners are published. _(public_company_claim)_
- **trial_offer** — The home page states the offer as free for 30 days. _(public_company_claim)_
- **setup_claim** — The home page also states setup in less than an hour. That is an unverified performance claim about implementation time, not a published product specification. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **access_gate** — Access is gated. Both Request Access buttons route to a Performology-owned Google Form rather than to self-serve signup, and the form's stated purpose is to schedule a live demo. _(public_company_claim)_
- **form_fields** — The request form requires email, company, first and last name, contact phone, postal code and company size, and optionally captures title, best contact time, website and how the visitor heard about Performology. _(public_company_claim)_
- **company_size_bands** — The form segments prospects by company size across six bands from 1-5 employees to 500+ employees, indicating the product is sold to organisations of widely different sizes rather than only single agencies. _(public_company_claim)_
- **form_positioning** — The form describes Performology as a cutting-edge Performance Management System and asks whether the visitor wants a system that will drive the right behaviour, motivate employees to sell more, and save time and money. _(public_company_claim)_
- **app_surface** — The product itself runs as a login-gated web application at app.performology.com, with password reset and a create-a-new-account path. _(public_company_claim)_
- **app_gate** — The login screen states that by continuing the user represents they are an authorised Performology user and intends to use the services in accordance with Performology terms and conditions. _(public_company_claim)_
- **phone** — The published sales contact is 800-617-0112. _(public_company_claim)_
- **site_scope** — The public website is a single page. Requests for /about, /features, /pricing, /contact, /how-it-works, /integrations, /privacy and /terms all returned 404, and the only outbound destinations are the demo request form and the application login. _(public_company_claim)_
- **site_vintage** — The home page footer carries a 2018 copyright and the site publishes no dated content, changelog or news, so the public material may not reflect the current product. _(public_company_claim)_
- **testimonial_roles** — Published testimonials are attributed to agency owners and to licensed producers and representatives, and one describes staff having daily, weekly and monthly goals with multiple compensation buckets. _(public_company_claim)_
- **testimonial_outcomes** — Testimonials on the home page claim specific outcomes: an agency moving from #27 to #1 in its state and #56 to #3 in its region, and another claiming more sales in two weeks than a typical full month. These are unverified customer outcome claims. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **testimonial_comp_calc** — A published testimonial states the system saves the owner time calculating staff compensation. Compensation calculation is implied by that testimonial rather than documented as a product feature on any page observed. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_

**Sources**

- Performology — Home — https://performology.com/ (seen 2026-09-09)
- Performology — Application login — https://app.performology.com/ (seen 2026-09-09)
- Performology — Request Access / demo request form — https://docs.google.com/forms/d/1WOfZCV3M3o9NUGV09wN3Qcwy1eJy141iHdGfuHGIM1w/viewform (seen 2026-09-09)

**Workbook fit**

| Session | Fit | Where it lands | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The Sales Sequence | primary | Position Definition & Focus %; Accountability | Per-seat SMART goals tied to a parent company's objectives, plus incentive compensation attached to those goals, is exactly the accountability structure this session asks an owner to install. The owner still writes the sales sequence and decides what the seat is worth. |
| Growth Through Service | conditional | Position Definition & Focus % | Goals and incentives can be defined for service seats as readily as sales seats, which supports defining what a service position is accountable for. Conditional because nothing on the site addresses retention, onboarding or the service experience itself. |
| The Operating System | supporting_direct | Your Agency Bible | Broadcasting what is expected, where the agency stands and what it needs to do is the visible half of an operating standard. Supporting rather than primary because the system carries the numbers, not the written processes behind them. |
| Commitment to Training | conditional | Assess the Training | Visible per-seat performance tells an owner where the gaps are, which is the assessment input to a training decision. Conditional because Performology publishes no curriculum, no scored gate and no training content of any kind. |
| Making It Rain | not_recommended | — | Nothing published by Performology addresses lead generation, marketing spend, channel attribution or opportunity distribution. Placing it in this session would mean inventing a capability the site does not claim. |
| The Body Session | not_recommended | — | Company research is deliberately excluded from the personal workbooks at runtime, regardless of who is in the seat. A vendor's commercial context has no place shaping a member's personal work. |

**Room exercises and measures**

- _The Sales Sequence_ exercise: Take one producer seat, write the parent company objective it rolls up to, then write the daily number that proves it.
- _The Sales Sequence_ measure: Percentage of producer seats with a written goal and a compensation consequence attached to it.
- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: Write the one number a service seat is accountable for, and decide whether it should carry an incentive.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Service seats with a written, measured goal, as a share of all service seats.
- _The Operating System_ exercise: Name the one expectation your team should be able to see without asking you, and decide where it gets broadcast.
- _The Operating System_ measure: Whether every staff member can state their current number without opening a spreadsheet or asking the owner.
- _Commitment to Training_ exercise: Pick the performance gap you would train against first, and decide how you would know the training worked.
- _Commitment to Training_ measure: Gaps identified from the scoreboard that actually turned into a scheduled training session.

**On these resource pages**

- **Formula Sales Sequence Resources** (/resources/sales-sequence)
  - Helps with: Employee performance management, goals, measurement and performance accountability.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves clearly defining, measuring and managing producer performance.
- **Operating System Resources** (/resources/operating-system)
  - Helps with: Employee performance management, goal setting, measurement and review cadence.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves defining what good looks like for a role and managing against it.

**Open questions**

- All pricing. No price, plan or seat cost is published anywhere on the public site; access runs through a demo request form.
- Which lead management, CRM and reporting tools Performology actually integrates with. The site claims a seamless integration platform but names no partner, protocol or API.
- Whether the product is still actively sold and developed. The footer carries a 2018 copyright, the site has no dated content, and the public surface is a single page.
- Company leadership, ownership, headquarters, founding date and team size. None is published.
- Which parent companies or carrier franchise systems the goal alignment is built around. The pitch assumes a parent company setting objectives but names none.
- Whether compensation is calculated inside the product. The only evidence is a customer testimonial, not a documented feature.
- The terms of the stated 30-day free offer: whether a card is required, what happens at day 31, and whether setup support is included.
- Any outcome, retention or production data. The only results language on the site is unverified customer testimonials.
- Whether Performology serves independent agencies at all, given the goal-alignment model is framed around a parent company.
- Only three company-owned pages exist to research: the single-page site, the login screen and the demo request form. This record is thinner than the other partner files for that reason, not through lack of searching.

**Activation notes**

- runtimeSurface: Partner-seat business workbooks only (s1, s2, s4, s5)
- identityStatus: awaiting_account_and_organization

Bind the exact existing partner organization before any write, then separately authorize the Firebase Functions release. Book mapping is editorial analysis, not partner-approved placement.

### 16. GOAL

- **Tier:** Bronze
- **Website:** https://checkoutgoal.com
- **Logo:** `/assets/sponsors/goal.svg`
- **Resource-page id:** `goal`
- **Category:** Insurance marketing technology: agent-branded quote funnels plus done-for-you paid advertising to generate the agent's own leads
- **Also known as:** Goal Platform, Goal Platform LLC, CheckOutGoal.com, QuoteFunnel
- **Research version:** 2026-09-09.1, reviewed 2026-09-09

**What we know (22 researched facts)**

- **identity** — GOAL is operated by Goal Platform LLC under the name CheckOutGoal.com. Its privacy policy states it services US residents only and does not offer services to residents outside the US. The agent terms observed were updated July 29, 2026 and the privacy policy August 26, 2026. _(public_company_claim)_
- **positioning** — GOAL positions itself against lead aggregators. Rather than selling an agent shared leads, it sells the agent a marketing technology platform to attract, capture and convert the agent's own leads under the agent's own branding. _(public_company_claim)_
- **quotefunnel** — The core product is the QuoteFunnel: a quote-request form the agent personalises with their own logo and branding. It can be placed on the agent's website, landing pages, email newsletters, social media ads and marketing materials, and GOAL states it can be live in minutes. _(public_company_claim)_
- **done_for_you_media** — GOAL also runs the advertising. It places the agent's ads on Google and Facebook and on what it calls exclusive Goal Platform Partner Traffic, so an agency with no website of its own can still run the funnel. _(public_company_claim)_
- **how_it_works** — The published sequence is four steps: the agent sets up ad creative with their logo and branding, chooses a target audience and sets a budget; a consumer finds the ad through search, Facebook or the partner network; the click lands on the agent's personalised funnel to complete a quote request; the agent receives an email that a new lead has arrived. _(public_company_claim)_
- **lines_supported** — GOAL states it supports agents who write auto, home, life, health, Medicare, commercial and renters. _(public_company_claim)_
- **pricing_model** — The commercial model in the agent terms is cost per click. Subject to the advertiser's assigned budget and specified CPC bids, GOAL displays the advertiser's links and ads on sites it manages or has contracted, and the advertiser pays a predetermined fee for each unique click as set out in the platform. No dollar figure is published anywhere on the site. _(public_company_claim)_
- **lead_ownership** — The agent terms state that ownership of all Leads generated by the Advertiser is retained JOINTLY by the Advertiser and Goal. This sits alongside marketing language about generating your own exclusive leads, and an agency should read the two together before assuming sole ownership. _(public_company_claim)_
- **lead_data_rights** — Once the advertiser has met its payment obligations for clicks, it receives full rights to use the lead data for insurance marketing consistent with the agreement and applicable law. If the advertiser rejects a lead or fails to pay for one, it forfeits all rights to data provided by GOAL or collected from that user, including ping data. _(public_company_claim)_
- **reporting** — GOAL publishes full transparency reporting on clicks, leads and calls received, click-through rates, and where the most valuable customers originate, so the agent can see where each marketing dollar went. _(public_company_claim)_
- **integrations** — GOAL states it is fully integrated with the most popular CRMs so leads and calls can be routed into the platform of the agent's choice. No named integration list was published on the pages observed. _(public_company_claim)_
- **account_management** — Dedicated account managers are included, described as helping a non-technical agent get set up and then optimise budget and conversions. _(public_company_claim)_
- **compliance_claims** — GOAL states it is SOC 2 Type 2, CMS and TCPA compliant, that consent is captured from all consumer submissions, and that each prospect is validated and screened for authenticity before delivery. The terms qualify the TCPA position: GOAL assures the advertiser that, where mandated by the applicable Insertion Order, leads were generated with clear and conspicuous disclosure that the consumer would receive future calls or texts. _(public_company_claim)_
- **advertiser_compliance_duty** — The agent terms require the advertiser to comply with all federal, state and local laws and industry best practices relevant to its business, including all laws governing the offer and sale of insurance products and any applicable licensing requirements. _(public_company_claim)_
- **call_recording** — GOAL's privacy policy states all calls to or from Goal Platform LLC, including call transfers, may be monitored and recorded, stored and analysed for an indefinite period. The advertiser consents to this and must obtain the consent of its own agents, employees and contractors who may be recorded. _(public_company_claim)_
- **contract_terms** — The agreement includes mandatory binding arbitration and a waiver of class actions, and states GOAL may modify the terms periodically with or without notice, with continued use treated as acceptance. The advertiser may not use GOAL's name or marks without written consent, while GOAL receives an unlimited royalty-free licence to display the advertiser's marks on its own sites. _(public_company_claim)_
- **data_practices** — The privacy policy states GOAL creates aggregate data from personal information, reserves the right to transfer and sell aggregate data for lawful purposes, shares internal reports with advertisers and partners, and discloses identifiers to service providers. Registration collects agency name, contact details and billing and payment information. _(public_company_claim)_
- **conversion_claim_unverified** — GOAL states its funnels help agents convert 5x more users than traditional sales funnels and that its forms have been proven to capture 5x more insurance prospects. No baseline, sample or period is given. This is a vendor outcome claim and was not verified. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **network_claim_unverified** — GOAL claims a network of over 10 million high-intent online insurance shoppers and over 15 years of experience in the insurance marketing industry. Both are undated marketing counts and were not verified. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **comparison_claim_unverified** — GOAL's Old Way versus GOAL Way comparison asserts that aggregator leads are shared with 5 to 10 agents, produce low contact rates and confused consumers, and come with contracts forcing continued spend. This is a characterisation of competitors, not a measured finding, and should not be repeated as fact. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **testimonial_unverified** — The homepage carries a single named testimonial from an independent agent describing GOAL as a game changer after mixed results with other lead vendors. One selected testimonial is not evidence of typical results. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **roi_claim_unverified** — GOAL describes a highly profitable ROI, a steady flow of exclusive leads and done-for-you marketing that keeps a team busy. These are unquantified marketing assertions with no published measurement. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_

**Sources**

- GOAL | Generate Your Own In-market Insurance Leads — https://checkoutgoal.com/ (seen 2026-09-09)
- How It Works | GOAL — https://checkoutgoal.com/how-it-works.html (seen 2026-09-09)
- Agent Terms & Conditions — Goal Platform LLC — https://checkoutgoal.com/terms-of-use.html (seen 2026-09-09)
- Privacy Policy — Goal Platform LLC — https://checkoutgoal.com/privacy-policy.html (seen 2026-09-09)

That is a marketing strategy decision, not just a distribution one. Note for the room: the terms say leads are owned jointly by the agency and GOAL, which is worth reading next to the exclusive-leads marketing. |

Conditional because these are contract facts, not a system GOAL installs for you. |

Conditional because GOAL publishes nothing about service after the sale and names no CRM it integrates with. |

**Room exercises and measures**

- _Making It Rain_ exercise: Decide what you would spend per month on ads that carry your own brand versus leads that carry someone else's, then say which one you would defend to your team.
- _Making It Rain_ measure: Cost per click, cost per submitted funnel form, and cost per bound policy, tracked separately from purchased leads.
- _The Operating System_ exercise: Name who in your agency reads the vendor terms when they change, and when they last did it.
- _The Operating System_ measure: Whether the ad budget owner, the terms reviewer and the staff call-recording disclosure are documented and current.
- _The Sales Sequence_ exercise: Put last month's funnel submissions next to quotes issued and find the gap.
- _The Sales Sequence_ measure: Funnel submissions to quotes issued, by producer.
- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: Open your funnel on a phone as a stranger would and decide whether the first screen sounds like your agency.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Whether funnel branding, first-contact script and welcome message tell one consistent story.

**On these resource pages**

- **Making It Rain Resources** (/resources/making-it-rain)
  - Helps with: A marketing platform for building your own funnels and capturing insurance prospects under your own brand.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves generating your own in-market prospects instead of buying them from a marketplace.

**Open questions**

- All pricing. The model is cost per click against an advertiser budget, but no CPC range, platform fee, setup fee, minimum budget or contract length is published anywhere on the site.
- Whether there is a separate software subscription for the QuoteFunnel platform on top of ad spend.
- Which CRMs GOAL integrates with. Integration is claimed generically with no named systems.
- What Goal Platform Partner Traffic actually is — which sites, and how consumers arrive there.
- The practical effect of the joint lead-ownership clause: whether GOAL may market to, resell or otherwise use a lead the agency generated and paid for.
- Whether there is a lead credit or return process. No return or credit policy was published on the pages observed.
- Whether the SOC 2 Type 2 attestation, CMS and TCPA compliance claims can be evidenced by a report or certificate on request.
- Any independent verification of the 5x conversion claim or the 10 million shopper network. Both are vendor-published and recorded here as unconfirmed.

**Activation notes**

- runtimeSurface: Partner-seat business workbooks only (s1, s2, s4, s5, s7)

### 17. Mav

- **Tier:** Bronze
- **Website:** https://hiremav.com
- **Logo:** `/assets/sponsors/mav.png`
- **Resource-page id:** `mav`
- **Category:** Conversational AI SMS assistant for insurance lead engagement, qualification and live transfer
- **Also known as:** Hire Mav, Mav Automation Ventures Inc, hiremav.com
- **Research version:** 2026-09-09.1, reviewed 2026-09-09

**Podcast interview**

- Guest: Matthew Black
- Vimeo id: 1221786634
- Contact: Visit Mav → https://hiremav.com
- Shown as: hiremav.com
- Note: Visit Mav to learn more and get started with Matthew and the team.

**What we know (25 researched facts)**

- **what_it_is** — Mav is described as an AI-powered insurance expert that engages leads with text messages to qualify, quote and connect prospects to agents. The site positions it explicitly as a replacement for running a call center. _(public_company_claim)_
- **legal_entity** — The site footer identifies the operating company as Mav Automation Ventures Inc. _(public_company_claim)_
- **three_steps** — Mav describes a three-step motion: Engage (reach out to leads instantly for speed-to-lead), Qualify (identify qualified leads and filter the rest into further follow-up), and Connect (integrate with your system to enable live call transfers to your team). _(public_company_claim)_
- **qualification_flow** — In an automated two-way text conversation Mav collects qualifying information, then nurtures leads with Smart Re-Engagements until they complete the process. Sales reps are notified when a lead is ready and the qualified lead plus its information is delivered into the CRM. _(public_company_claim)_
- **live_transfer** — Mav Party Lines lets a qualified lead start a phone call directly from the text thread. Mav calls the lead from the same number they have been texting, simultaneously calls an available sales rep, and joins the two once both confirm availability. _(public_company_claim)_
- **live_transfer_fallback** — If the lead or the rep is unavailable at transfer time, Mav immediately texts the lead to find the next best time for a call and continues updating statuses in the CRM. _(public_company_claim)_
- **feature_list** — Published feature list: Smart Intents (responds when a lead goes off script), Smart Texting Windows (send during hours leads are most likely to respond), Auto Re-Engagements (nurture messages over days, weeks and months), a REST API, a Whitelabel Persona, live transfer calls, a Zapier app, and a maintained NLP engine. _(public_company_claim)_
- **fully_managed** — Mav is described as fully managed rather than self-serve: the vendor trains, maintains and improves the assistant, and states that every new skill and improvement is automatically integrated for existing customers. _(public_company_claim)_
- **whitelabel_voice** — Mav can adapt to a customer's brand voice, or the vendor can create a custom digital assistant persona, so the texting identity does not have to be the vendor's. _(public_company_claim)_
- **playbooks** — Mav says it is trained on industry-vetted playbooks and that a customer can upload their own training manual so the business is represented accurately. _(public_company_claim)_
- **curated_playbooks** — The company states its approach is curated rather than open-ended: Mav runs on playbooks created by Mav and co-authored by the customer, improved daily from conversation data collected across its customer base. _(public_company_claim)_
- **concurrency** — Mav states it can hold conversations with an unlimited number of leads simultaneously, so scaling volume does not require additional hires. _(public_company_claim)_
- **integrations** — Mav publishes one-click integrations with Salesforce, HubSpot, Tableau, Slack and Microsoft Teams, plus official Zapier support and a REST API. The site states all integrations are included free with all Mav plans. _(public_company_claim)_
- **api_control** — The API lets a customer programmatically trigger Playbooks and subscribe to outcomes, so Mav can be started and read from an existing system rather than only from its own interface. _(public_company_claim)_
- **copilot** — Copilot is described as a real-time view into Mav's conversations, showing full chat transcriptions and the decision logic behind each response, so an owner can see exactly what the assistant is saying to their leads. _(public_company_claim)_
- **copilot_training** — Copilot proactively flags the operator when Mav is unsure how to respond to a lead. The operator supplies the preferred answer, and Mav stores and trains on that response for the next lead who asks the same question. _(public_company_claim)_
- **cognitive** — Mav Cognitive is described as adding reasoning capability on top of automation: it can read call transcriptions, distill conversations into summaries, and interpret lead responses that do not follow an expected format, operating within stated guardrails intended to prevent errors or hallucinations. _(public_company_claim)_
- **origin_2016** — The company states Mav was founded in 2016 with a mission to democratize AI, and that it powers Mav on its own MIT-licensed open-source conversational framework, Stealth. _(public_company_claim)_
- **sms_consent_notice** — Mav's own lead form carries an SMS consent disclosure — providing a phone number is stated to be agreement to receive text messages, with message and data rates possibly applying — and the site publishes a Messaging Policy, a Compliance Center and a public status page. _(public_company_claim)_
- **hosting** — Mav's published security overview states servers are in AWS's Virginia region with data written to multiple disks, backed up daily and stored across availability zones; encryption at rest is AWS AES-256 disk encryption with AWS-managed keys, transit uses SSL certificates issued by Cloudflare, and uploaded files sit in private S3 buckets requiring one-time, time-limited access tokens. _(public_company_claim)_
- **personnel_controls** — The security overview states all employees and contractors sign confidentiality agreements, remote production access is limited to those who need it, all account access is logged by IP, and worker machines require full-disk encryption plus VPN off-site. It also states plainly that background checks are not performed on workers. _(public_company_claim)_
- **payments** — Payments are handled by Stripe; the security page states payment details bypass Mav's servers entirely and never appear in its logs. _(public_company_claim)_
- **security_page_age** — The security overview is stamped 'Last updated: November 5, 2020'. Whether those controls still describe current practice, and whether Mav holds any third-party audit such as SOC 2, was not established on the pages observed. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **homepage_outcome_stats** — The homepage advertises 50% lower cost of service, a 30% higher lead conversion rate and 24% lower cost per acquisition. These are undated vendor outcome claims with no stated methodology, sample or period. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **case_study_claims** — A 'Real Results' block claims 78% of inbound leads qualified and 11.7x ROI for 1-800-LAWFIRM, 2.5x ROI for Homebay Financial, and 1,271 mortgage leads qualified at 6.7x ROI for an unnamed leading mortgage provider. All are undated, and none of the three named examples is an insurance agency. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_

**Sources**

- Mav — AI SMS Assistant for Insurance Leads — https://hiremav.com/ (seen 2026-09-09)
- Why Choose Mav AI Texting Assistant — https://hiremav.com/why (seen 2026-09-09)
- Automate Lead Qualification — https://hiremav.com/qualification (seen 2026-09-09)
- Automated Live Transfer Text-to-Calls — https://hiremav.com/live-transfer (seen 2026-09-09)
- Our Conversational AI Technology — https://hiremav.com/technology (seen 2026-09-09)
- AI CRM & API Integrations — https://hiremav.com/integrate (seen 2026-09-09)
- AI Conversation Management — Copilot — https://hiremav.com/copilot (seen 2026-09-09)
- Cognitive AI Platform — https://hiremav.com/cognitive (seen 2026-09-09)
- Mav Security Overview — https://hiremav.com/security (seen 2026-09-09)

| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Making It Rain | primary | The Opportunity Distribution | Mav sits on the leads an agency already buys and decides which ones reach a human. Instant outreach, timed re-engagement over months and unlimited concurrent conversations change how opportunity is distributed to the team. It does not decide what to spend or where leads come from. |
| The Sales Sequence | supporting_direct | Position Definition & Focus % | Handing producers only qualified, warm-transferred conversations changes what the producer seat is for and where its focus percentage should sit. Copilot transcripts show what was said before the handoff. The sequence the producer runs after the transfer is still the agency's to write. |

Copilot's train-once-reuse loop keeps that written standard current. This documents the lead-handling lane only, not the rest of the operation. |

Two-way texting and CRM write-back could carry a service touch, but no service, renewal or retention workflow was published on the pages observed, and existing-customer texting carries its own consent posture. |

| My 2027 Map | reference_only | — | The Map compiles decisions already made in the other sessions; company research is not loaded here and should not introduce anything new. |

**Room exercises and measures**

- _Making It Rain_ exercise: Write down what happens to a lead in its first five minutes today, then in its first thirty days, and mark every step no human actually performs.
- _Making It Rain_ measure: Contact rate and qualified-transfer rate per lead source, before and after automated follow-up.
- _The Sales Sequence_ exercise: Split your producer's day into chasing versus closing, then decide what percentage of the seat should survive if chasing were removed.
- _The Sales Sequence_ measure: Share of producer conversations that arrive as accepted live transfers rather than outbound dials.
- _The Operating System_ exercise: Take the ten questions leads ask most and write the one approved answer for each, then decide who owns updating them.
- _The Operating System_ measure: Number of Copilot escalations per week, and whether that count falls as answers are captured.
- _Commitment to Training_ exercise: Read five real lead transcripts out loud in the room and mark every objection nobody on the team was trained to handle.
- _Commitment to Training_ measure: Transcripts reviewed per week by a manager, and objections added to team training as a result.
- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: List the service moments you would want texted, then check which of them you have written consent to text about.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Nothing yet — confirm with the vendor whether existing-book outreach is in scope before setting one.

**On these resource pages**

- **Making It Rain Resources** (/resources/making-it-rain)
  - Helps with: Automated lead engagement, follow-up and qualification before opportunities reach the producer.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves opportunity going unworked because producers cannot get to all of it.
- **Formula Sales Sequence Resources** (/resources/sales-sequence)
  - Helps with: Automated lead engagement, follow-up and qualification before opportunities reach the producer.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves producers spending too much time chasing prospects who are not ready to talk.

**Open questions**

- Pricing. No price, plan tier or minimum is published on the pages observed; the site routes to Get Started and a contact form. Every figure is quoted privately and is deliberately absent here.
- Contract shape — term length, message volume limits, per-conversation or per-transfer billing, and whether live transfer costs extra.
- Whether Mav holds any third-party security audit such as SOC 2. The published security overview is dated November 5, 2020 and claims controls, not certifications.
- TCPA and 10DLC posture in practice. A Messaging Policy and Compliance Center are published but were not opened, and who holds consent for a given lead was not established.
- Whether Mav works an agency's existing book — renewals, service, winback — or only new inbound and purchased leads. Every page observed describes new-lead engagement.
- Named insurance-agency references. The three published case studies are a law firm, a mortgage company and an unnamed mortgage provider.
- Which AMS or agency-specific systems Mav integrates with. Published one-click integrations are Salesforce, HubSpot, Tableau, Slack and Microsoft Teams.
- Implementation timeline and what the agency must supply. The site says 'launch in days' without stating what that requires.
- Whether any of the published outcome statistics reflect insurance agencies, and over what period they were measured.

**Activation notes**

- runtimeSurface: Partner-seat business workbooks only (s1, s2, s4, s5, s7)
- identityStatus: no_partner_organization_bound
- disclosure: Research compiled from Mav's own public website only. No representative was contacted and no claim was independently verified.

### 18. SmartFinancial

- **Tier:** Bronze
- **Website:** https://smartfinancial.com
- **Logo:** `/assets/sponsors/smart-financial.png`
- **Resource-page id:** `smartfinancial`
- **Category:** Insurance comparison marketplace selling leads, calls and clicks to agents and carriers
- **Also known as:** SmartFinancial.com Insurance LLC, SmartFinancial Insurance, Contactability, SmartFinancial Agents
- **Research version:** 2026-09-09.1, reviewed 2026-09-09

**What we know (24 researched facts)**

- **identity** — SmartFinancial is an insurance comparison marketplace founded in 2012 by Lev Barinskiy, who began his career as an insurance agent, and CTO Nate Kropp. The legal entity is SmartFinancial.com Ins LLC, doing business as SmartFinancial Insurance, with offices in Newport Beach, California and Columbus, Ohio. _(public_company_claim)_
- **what_is_sold** — To agents, SmartFinancial sells leads, calls and clicks. Consumer lines covered are auto, home, renters, life, health, Medicare, commercial and motorcycle, in all 50 states. _(public_company_claim)_
- **sourcing** — SmartFinancial states a majority of its traffic is generated on SmartFinancial.com itself, where consumers shopping for insurance answer questions and are then verified for authenticity before being connected to an insurance professional. The consumer side is a comparison funnel, not a referral directory. _(public_company_claim)_
- **bid_model** — Pricing works as a bid. SmartFinancial automatically recommends a bid for the calls, leads and clicks an agent receives, and the agent can raise the bid to win more traffic in competitive areas. The agent pays only for calls, leads and clicks actually received. _(public_company_claim)_
- **no_contract** — SmartFinancial states there are no long-term commitments or contracts for agents. _(public_company_claim)_
- **billing** — Enrollment requires a deposit on a credit or debit card. The account auto-refills when the balance runs low, and the agent can adjust the auto-rebill setting, fund the account manually and update card details from the Billing tab. _(public_company_claim)_
- **profiles** — Buying is organised into profiles. An agent creates a call or lead profile, and each profile carries its own settings; there is also an account-level global maximum for leads and a separate global maximum for calls. _(public_company_claim)_
- **controls_volume** — Per-profile volume control is a Calls/Leads per day field, with account-wide global caps in Settings. A profile can also be paused outright from the Profile Settings box. _(public_company_claim)_
- **controls_geo_filters** — Geography is set per profile by adding or editing area codes or selecting an entire state. Additional targeting is set through an Add/Edit Filters control inside Profile Settings. _(public_company_claim)_
- **controls_calls** — For calls the agent sets hours of operation, a concurrency cap and the transfer phone number, either per profile or account-wide, from the Call Settings tab. Hours of operation is the intended way to control call availability rather than pausing. _(public_company_claim)_
- **evidence** — Received leads and calls appear on a dashboard where the agent clicks through to contact details, and call recordings are played from the same dashboard. Transaction reports are downloadable from the Billing tab. _(public_company_claim)_
- **team_access** — Additional users and additional notification email addresses can be added to the account from the Settings tab, so more than one person can work the flow. _(public_company_claim)_
- **lead_fields** — SmartFinancial publishes sample prospect data. The auto sample carries contact details, existing coverage and current carrier with policy expiry, desired limits and deductibles, driver detail including licence status, occupation, education, credit rating, home ownership, DUI and SR-22 flags, and vehicle detail including year, make, model, VIN, use, annual miles and garaging. _(public_company_claim)_
- **returns_window** — Lead return requests are submitted up to 10 days after delivery with a detailed explanation, and the policy states requests must be submitted after 7 days of receipt. Agents with auto-rebill turned off must contact Account Management to return. Accepted returns are credited to the account balance; a cash refund is never permitted. _(public_company_claim)_
- **returns_reasons** — Published credit reasons include all numbers disconnected, fax or wrong number; a duplicate within 30 days; fictitious or materially inaccurate contact information; a lead outside the agent's geography; a test lead; a language barrier; a lead already insured with the captive agent's own company; and a system or filter error. _(public_company_claim)_
- **returns_denied** — Published reasons a return will not be accepted include the same lead arriving from another source, an underwriting decline, the consumer having already bought elsewhere, the agent not having set a filter to exclude unwanted leads, no policy written, unanswered calls that reach voicemail, and split zip codes that legitimately route calls from a neighbouring area code. Leads bought with promo credits are not eligible. _(public_company_claim)_
- **returns_obligations** — Once a return is requested the agent must immediately stop using the lead and delete every copy, including copies exported into a CRM. Failure to do so results in immediate denial. SmartFinancial reserves the right to investigate and to decline in its sole discretion, and a high overall return rate can trigger an account audit. _(public_company_claim)_
- **support** — Accounts come with a dedicated account management team and a published support phone line and email address. _(public_company_claim)_
- **referral_offer** — SmartFinancial publishes a referral offer of $50 worth of free leads or calls for every agent referred. This is a promotional credit, not a published price for the product itself. _(public_company_claim)_
- **ramp_expectation** — SmartFinancial states an agent may receive leads and calls on the first day, and that timing depends on bid, territory and how competitive the area is. It publishes no guaranteed volume. _(public_company_claim)_
- **network_counts_unverified** — Marketing counts published without a stated measurement method: a network of 6,500+ insurance partners, over 15 million shoppers this year on the agent page and 30m+ insurance shoppers for 2025 on the about page, 320+ employees, over 14 years serving the industry, and the self-description as the #1 Insurance Marketplace. Not independently verified. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **premium_claim_unverified** — SmartFinancial states its proprietary technology has helped insurers add over a billion dollars in premiums annually and that it sorts through over 200 insurance companies for consumers. These are vendor outcome and scale claims and were not verified. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **testimonials_unverified** — The agent page carries named agent testimonials describing prompt call refunds, good prospect quality and, in one case, growth of 16 households in a month. These are selected testimonials, not measured results. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **cost_per_policy_claim_unverified** — The agent page advertises a reliable cost per policy and an agent-friendly credit policy. Neither is quantified anywhere on the public pages, so both are recorded as unconfirmed marketing language. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_

The bid model in particular makes the price of an opportunity a decision rather than a fixed cost, which is exactly what this session forces an owner to confront. |

Those are operating rules that must be written down and owned by someone, or the agency quietly forfeits credits it is entitled to. |

Conditional because everything after that first contact is the agency's own service standard. |

**Room exercises and measures**

- _Making It Rain_ exercise: Write your current bid and daily cap per profile, then decide the cost per bound policy above which you would cut the bid instead of raising it.
- _Making It Rain_ measure: Cost per policy by profile, tracked against the bid you set.
- _The Sales Sequence_ exercise: Pull three call recordings from last week and score them against the one standard you wish your team ran.
- _The Sales Sequence_ measure: Recordings reviewed per producer per week, and answered rate inside published hours of operation.
- _The Operating System_ exercise: Name the person who owns lead returns, then write the trigger, the deadline and the delete step as one rule on one page.
- _The Operating System_ measure: Returns submitted inside the window as a percentage of returnable leads, and credits approved.
- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: Circle the three fields on the sample lead your team never uses, and decide what would change if they did.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Percentage of first contacts that reference the prospect's current carrier and renewal date.

**On these resource pages**

- **Making It Rain Resources** (/resources/making-it-rain)
  - Helps with: Insurance leads and calls from a consumer quote-comparison marketplace.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves buying opportunity that matches a specific appetite or geography.

**Open questions**

- All pricing. Bids are recommended by the platform and vary by market; no dollar figure, minimum deposit amount or bid floor is published.
- The full filter catalogue. The FAQ says filters are added per profile but does not list which filters exist for each line.
- How many agents a shared lead is sold to, and whether any product is exclusive. Distribution is not stated anywhere on the pages observed.
- The return-policy timing language is internally contradictory as published: requests may be submitted up to 10 days after delivery, and also must be submitted after 7 days of receipt. The intended window should be confirmed with the company before an agency writes a rule around it.
- Which CRMs SmartFinancial integrates with. The agent pages mention exporting leads to a CRM but publish no integration list.
- What triggers the account audit for a high return rate, and what the audit does.
- Any independent verification of lead quality, contact rate or close rate. Every performance figure on the site is vendor-published and is recorded here as unconfirmed.

### 19. SmarketingMail

- **Tier:** Bronze
- **Website:** https://smarketingmail.com
- **Logo:** `/assets/sponsors/smarketing-mail.png`
- **Resource-page id:** `smarketingmail`
- **Category:** Direct mail vendor — exclusive-ZIP personalised home insurance mailers for P&C agents
- **Also known as:** Smarketing, Smarketing Mail, Inc, SmarketingMail
- **Research version:** 2026-09-09.1, reviewed 2026-09-09

**What we know (22 researched facts)**

- **entity** — The operating entity is named in the privacy policy as Smarketing Mail, Inc. The published address is 1000 North Walnut Creek Drive, Mansfield, Texas 76063, with hours of Monday to Friday 9:00am to 5:00pm and closed weekends. _(public_company_claim)_
- **positioning** — The single published product is home insurance direct mail for P&C insurance agents. The tagline is "Smarter Direct Mail for Insurance Agents" and the positioning is "Built by agents - for agents". _(public_company_claim)_
- **operator_led** — The company states it is run by a top P&C agent, and argues that having skin in the game means it innovates, adjusts and adapts faster than mail providers who are not agents. _(public_company_claim)_
- **zip_exclusivity** — ZIP code exclusivity is the central published commitment: an agent locks in ZIP codes, and Smarketing states it will not send the same letter for multiple agents in the same area, describing that practice at other providers as crushing contact rates. The company says it sacrifices potential profit to hold that line. _(public_company_claim)_
- **minimum_volume** — The published minimum commitment is 3,000 mail pieces per month. _(public_company_claim)_
- **pricing_structure** — Pricing is described as competitive tier pricing — the more an agent sends, the more they save — with the actual rate depending on ZIP code availability and lead volume with filters. No dollar figure is published anywhere on the site. _(public_company_claim)_
- **qr_landing_page** — The response mechanism is a QR code that opens a landing page pre-filled with the recipient's own information; the consumer only enters a phone number and submits. An image of the recipient's home can be added to the piece to draw attention. _(public_company_claim)_
- **process** — The published five-step process is: lock in ZIP codes and discuss filters together; Smarketing provides the leads; the agent optionally scrubs out existing customers; the agent and Smarketing jointly determine the mock quote premium and coverages; Smarketing prints and mails, and the agent takes the inbound calls. _(public_company_claim)_
- **agent_inputs** — What the agent has to supply is explicit and unusually short: ZIP code choices, filter preferences, the mock quote premium and coverages, and optionally a suppression list of existing customers. Everything after that — data, print, mail — is done for them. _(public_company_claim)_
- **no_lead_purchase** — Smarketing supplies the prospect list itself as part of the service rather than asking the agent to bring one, and the agent's only lead-side task is optional suppression of current customers. _(public_company_claim)_
- **zip_map_intake** — Pre-sale intake is a ZIP code availability map: an agent submits name, email, mobile phone and office postal code and receives a map link showing where they can send exclusive personalised offers. A booking widget and a direct line, 817-948-7746, are also published. _(public_company_claim)_
- **application_exists** — The privacy policy states that Smarketing Mail owns and operates both smarketingmail.com and an application at app.smarketingmail.com, and describes registered users with usernames, passwords and profiles — so there is a client-facing portal separate from the marketing site. _(public_company_claim)_
- **account_data** — Registration for the application collects username, password, name, address, phone number, email address and company name. The policy states that name, company name and contact information may be visible to other users through the profile. _(public_company_claim)_
- **billing** — Billing information collected includes credit card number or ACH banking information, and the policy notes additional financial information may be required to verify identity and to bill for use of the services. _(public_company_claim)_
- **community_features** — The privacy policy references message boards, in-app services and member-only areas where users can post and leave feedback for other members, and warns that anything posted there can be read by other users. _(public_company_claim)_
- **referral_program** — A referral program exists: a user can submit a friend's email address, which triggers a one-time invitation email. The policy states the address is only stored if the referral signs up. _(public_company_claim)_
- **contact_person** — The published point of contact on the site is a named individual, Brittany, reachable at the company email address and phone number listed on the homepage. _(public_company_claim)_
- **outcome_claims** — The homepage claims higher closing ratios, higher contact rates and better pricing than competitor mail providers. No figure, period or method is published behind any of the three. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **adoption_claim** — The site claims that most of the top P&C agents with every carrier use the service. The claim is undated, unquantified and was not confirmed. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **competitor_claim** — The site asserts that other mail providers send the same letter for multiple agents and that this crushes contact rates. That is a characterisation of competitors, not a verified observation. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **app_unreachable** — app.smarketingmail.com, named in the privacy policy as the company's application, failed DNS resolution when loaded on 2026-09-09. Whether the portal is live, renamed or retired was not confirmed. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **missing_pages** — The footer links to Terms and Conditions and a Link Policy, but both destinations return the site's 404 page. The published contract terms are therefore not actually readable on the site. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_

**Sources**

- Smarketing — Home insurance direct mail for agents — https://smarketingmail.com/ (seen 2026-09-09)
- Smarketing Mail — Privacy Policy — https://smarketingmail.com/static_pages/privacy_policy (seen 2026-09-09)

The five-step process makes clear exactly which choices stay with the owner: which ZIPs, which filters, what premium to show. |

That is a defined call opening the team can be held to. The sequence itself, and who answers, is still the owner's to write. |

Conditional because the process belongs to the vendor and only its input edge lands in your operation. |

**Room exercises and measures**

- _Making It Rain_ exercise: Pull your ZIP availability map first, then decide which ZIPs you would defend for twelve months at 3,000 pieces a month.
- _Making It Rain_ measure: Pieces mailed, QR and call responses, and quoted households per ZIP per month.
- _The Sales Sequence_ exercise: Write the opening line for a call to someone who already saw your mock quote, and decide the callback window you will enforce.
- _The Sales Sequence_ measure: QR submissions contacted within the callback window, and quoted, by producer.
- _The Operating System_ exercise: Name who owns the monthly ZIP, filter and suppression-list decisions, and put the date it happens on the calendar.
- _The Operating System_ measure: Monthly drop inputs submitted on time, out of twelve.
- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: Try to produce your current customer suppression list in ten minutes. Whatever blocks you is the real finding.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Time to produce a clean current-customer list, and duplicate or stale records found in it.

**On these resource pages**

- **Making It Rain Resources** (/resources/making-it-rain)
  - Helps with: Insurance direct mail with exclusive zip codes, list filters and QR-code landing pages, run by a P&C agent.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves owning a mail territory and turning mailed offers into inbound quote requests.

**Open questions**

- All pricing. Tier pricing is described but no cost per piece, per thousand or per ZIP is published; the rate depends on ZIP availability and filters and is quoted privately.
- Contract length, cancellation terms and what happens to a locked ZIP if an agent pauses. The footer links to Terms and Conditions and a Link Policy, but both return 404.
- How long ZIP exclusivity lasts, whether it is exclusive across all carriers or only within one, and what triggers its release.
- The available filters. The process says filters are discussed but never names them — no age, home value, tenure, renewal-date or claims criteria are published.
- Where the mailing data comes from, how it is refreshed, and whether the agent may keep or re-use it.
- The mail piece itself. Format, size, envelope treatment, personalisation depth and whether the design is customised per agency are not shown.
- Reporting. Nothing is published about what an agent sees after a drop — response counts, QR submissions, call volume or attribution.
- How QR submissions and inbound calls reach the agent, and whether they land in a CRM, an inbox or the portal.
- Whether app.smarketingmail.com is currently live. It is named in the privacy policy but did not resolve when loaded.
- Carrier and captive-brand compliance review of the mock quote premium and coverages shown on the mailer — material, since the agent supplies those numbers.
- Geographic coverage. The company is Texas-based and the intake form asks for an office postal code, but no list of served states is published.
- The site is a single page plus a privacy policy. There are no service, pricing, sample, case study or FAQ pages to corroborate the offering.
