### 20. Quote Nerds

- **Tier:** Bronze
- **Website:** https://quotenerds.com
- **Logo:** `/assets/sponsors/quote-nerds.png`
- **Resource-page id:** `quote-nerds`
- **Category:** Insurance lead generation: exclusive auto and home leads, live call transfers and local inbound-call marketing
- **Also known as:** QuoteNerds, Quote Nerds LLC, Nerd Rocket
- **Research version:** 2026-09-09.1, reviewed 2026-09-09

**What we know (22 researched facts)**

- **identity** — Quote Nerds is a Pennsylvania limited liability company (Quote Nerds LLC) that describes itself in its own terms as an online advertising publisher and lead generator/aggregator. Its published address is 134 Three Degree Rd, Pittsburgh, PA 15237. _(public_company_claim)_
- **what_is_sold** — An agent buys two things Quote Nerds calls Deliverables: consumer leads and live call transfers, sold for auto and home insurance. A separate product, Nerd Rocket, sells local inbound calls generated through the agency's own Google Business Profile. _(public_company_claim)_
- **exclusivity** — The terms state that on full payment and delivery the buyer receives sole and exclusive ownership of the Deliverables, and that Quote Nerds will not re-sell, re-license or reuse them without the buyer's prior written consent. This is the contractual basis for the site's exclusive-lead marketing. _(public_company_claim)_
- **portal** — Buying runs through a self-service portal at leads.quotenerds.com, powered by Jangl. The portal advertises control over lead and call purchasing, automatic de-duplication so the same lead is not paid for twice, real-time campaign optimisation the agent can change on the fly, and online payment. _(public_company_claim)_
- **filters_and_account_management** — Filters are customised to the agency and adjusted over time. Quote Nerds publishes a dedicated account manager with a monthly check-in to review progress and re-tune filters or shift campaign focus. _(public_company_claim)_
- **onboarding_process** — The published process is three steps: sign up, have campaigns built around the agency's stated targeting, then meet the account manager monthly to review results and adjust. _(public_company_claim)_
- **volume_tiers** — Quote Nerds publishes four lead-volume stages rather than packages by price: Launch & Learn at 4-7 leads per day, Scale & Optimize at 20-30 per day, Expand & Establish at 40-60 per day, and Dominate & Innovate at 80-100 per day, each described with the operational posture it expects of the agency. _(public_company_claim)_
- **payment_terms** — Purchases are due in full at time of order in US dollars via prepaid account balance, US-issued credit card, ACH or wire. The prepaid balance is deducted automatically and it is the buyer's responsibility to keep it funded. Delivery may be suspended or access terminated for non-payment, and all payments are non-refundable once received. _(public_company_claim)_
- **invalid_deliverables** — A claimed invalid Deliverable — duplicate, expired, fraudulent, or missing required fields — must be reported in writing within ten business days of receipt or it is deemed valid and non-disputable. Quote Nerds will supply supporting data such as timestamp, IP, URL and call recording on request, and its own good-faith determination after joint investigation controls the outcome. _(public_company_claim)_
- **expired_transfers** — The terms define an Expired Lead as one more than two minutes old at transfer, and commit only to commercially reasonable efforts to avoid delivering them. All Deliverables are sold as is, with no warranty as to consumer content, accuracy or facts. _(public_company_claim)_
- **consent_model** — Quote Nerds states it obtains or procures prior express consent from consumers at the point of inquiry, and that consumers may reject consent for ongoing communications. Leads without ongoing consent are delivered marked One Time Contact Only. _(public_company_claim)_
- **buyer_compliance_burden** — The terms place all post-delivery consent risk on the buying agency: determine whether additional consent is needed for its own calling technology and jurisdiction, obtain it at its own expense, honour revocations, scrub National and state Do-Not-Call lists immediately before contact, keep consent and compliance records for at least five years, and notify Quote Nerds within 24 hours of any consumer complaint or regulatory inquiry. _(public_company_claim)_
- **insurance_requirement** — Buyers covenant to carry commercial general liability and E&O insurance with TCPA-specific coverage of at least $5,000,000 per occurrence and $10,000,000 aggregate, naming Quote Nerds as additional insured, with certificates provided on request. This is a real cost of doing business with them and is easy to miss. _(public_company_claim)_
- **no_incentivised_traffic** — Quote Nerds states it does not use, and will not accept requests for, sweepstakes, rewards, points or any other compensation to induce consumers to submit inquiries. _(public_company_claim)_
- **nerd_rocket** — Nerd Rocket is a managed local-search program built around the agency's Google Business Profile, sold as exclusive inbound calls. Published operating promises are launch within 24 hours of sign-up and results within 60 days of launch, with active monthly management and content creation on the profile. _(public_company_claim)_
- **nerd_rocket_guarantee** — Quote Nerds publishes a guarantee on Nerd Rocket of one closed call per month or the next month is free. The terms of how a closed call is verified are not published. _(public_company_claim)_
- **terms_currency** — The lead and call transfer terms observed are dated effective March 6, 2026, and cite recent case law on consent, indicating the compliance language is actively maintained rather than boilerplate. _(public_company_claim)_
- **lines_covered** — Published lead lines are auto and home insurance, marketed to both solo independent agents and larger agencies. No life, health, Medicare or commercial lead product was published on the pages observed. _(public_company_claim)_
- **quality_claims_unverified** — Recurring marketing language — high-converting, high-quality, high-intent, exclusive, TCPA compliant, saves time and money, reduces agent burnout, increases monthly sales — is vendor assertion with no published measurement behind it. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **nerd_rocket_results_unverified** — Quote Nerds states that on average its Google Business Profile Nerd Rocket customers see a 13.4% increase in call volume. This is a vendor outcome claim with no stated sample, period or method, and was not verified. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **case_studies_unverified** — The Nerd Rocket page carries four unnamed agency anecdotes — two with the program in Kansas and Pennsylvania, two without it in Georgia — presented as call-volume charts. No agency, date range or figures are identified, so none of it is confirmable. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **tenure_bars_unreadable** — The homepage shows a tenure graphic implying results improve at 3 months, 6 months, 1 year and 2 years or more, but every value rendered as 0% when the page was loaded. No figure should be attributed to it. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_

**Room exercises and measures**

- _Making It Rain_ exercise: Put your current daily lead volume against the four published stages and say honestly which stage your team can actually work today.
- _Making It Rain_ measure: Cost per bound policy for bought exclusive leads versus cost per bound policy for Nerd Rocket inbound calls.
- _The Operating System_ exercise: Name who owns DNC scrubbing, who owns the ten-day dispute window, and where the five years of consent records physically live. If any answer is 'the owner', that is the finding.
- _The Operating System_ measure: Whether the compliance owner, the dispute deadline and the record location are written, assigned and current.
- _The Sales Sequence_ exercise: Decide the daily lead volume one producer can genuinely work, then divide your stage target by it and see how many seats you actually need.
- _The Sales Sequence_ measure: Leads worked per producer per day against the volume stage you bought.
- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: Decide what your team does differently the moment a lead arrives marked One Time Contact Only.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Whether One Time Contact Only leads are routed to a different, documented handling path.

**On these resource pages**

- **Making It Rain Resources** (/resources/making-it-rain)
  - Helps with: Outbound home and auto insurance leads plus inbound insurance calls sold to agents.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves adding quotable opportunity in the lines you actually want to write.

**Open questions**

- All pricing. No cost per lead, cost per transfer, minimum deposit, or Nerd Rocket monthly fee is published anywhere on the site; the volume stages are described without prices.
- The filter catalogue. Filters are said to be customised per agency but no list of available filters is published for either auto or home.
- The tenure graphic on the homepage implies improving results at 3, 6, 12 and 24+ months but rendered every value as 0%. The intended figures are unknown and none should be quoted.
- How Nerd Rocket verifies a closed call for the one-closed-call-per-month guarantee, and what the next-month-free credit is worth.
- Whether live call transfers are exclusive in the same way leads are, and how the two-minute expiry is measured in practice.
- Contract length, minimum spend and cancellation terms for either product.
- Any independent verification of lead quality, contact rate or close rate. Every performance figure on the site is vendor-published and is recorded here as unconfirmed.
- The site also serves live pages from a developer staging domain (forgeyourapp.com/quotenerds-new). Which is canonical was not confirmed; only quotenerds.com pages were used here.

**Activation notes**

- runtimeSurface: Partner-seat business workbooks only (s1, s2, s4, s5, s7)
- identityStatus: no_representative_checked

### 21. Ivantage

- **Tier:** Bronze
- **Website:** https://www.ivantageinsurance.ca
- **Logo:** `/assets/sponsors/ivantage.png`
- **Resource-page id:** `ivantage`
- **Category:** Independent property and casualty insurance brokerage in Canada, part of the Allstate Canada Group
- **Also known as:** Ivantage Insurance Brokers, Ivantage Insurance Brokers Inc., Ivantage Insurance
- **Research version:** 2026-09-09.1, reviewed 2026-09-09

**What we know (22 researched facts)**

- **identity** — Ivantage Insurance Brokers is an independent insurance brokerage operating in Canada, described on its own site as part of the Allstate Insurance Company of Canada Group of Companies and staffed by trusted, knowledgeable brokers. _(public_company_claim)_
- **footprint** — Ivantage states that it conducts business in Alberta, New Brunswick, Nova Scotia and Ontario. No other provinces or territories were named on the pages observed. _(public_company_claim)_
- **ownership** — Ivantage Insurance Brokers Inc. is stated to be a wholly owned subsidiary of Allstate Insurance Company of Canada. Together with Pembridge Insurance Company, Pafco Insurance Company, Esurance Insurance Company of Canada and Allstate Life Insurance Company of Canada it forms the Allstate Canada Group, which is part of The Allstate Corporation. _(public_company_claim)_
- **trademark** — The site carries the notice that the Allstate trademark is owned by Allstate Insurance Company and used under licence by Allstate Insurance Company of Canada. _(public_company_claim)_
- **contact** — Published contact points are a toll-free line at 1-855-622-2233, a local line at 905-948-3599, a fax at 905-513-0607 and the email address info@ivantageinsurance.ca. _(public_company_claim)_
- **lines** — Ivantage describes both Personal Lines and Commercial Lines product offerings and states it continues to look for ways to enhance those offerings. _(public_company_claim)_
- **positioning** — The brokerage positions itself on customized solutions, individual attention, a hands-on unrushed approach where no question goes unanswered, and fast efficient claims handling with access to vendors that provide guaranteed repairs in writing. These are self-described service commitments, not measured results. _(public_company_claim)_
- **claims_intake** — Ivantage publishes two ways to report a claim: directly to the brokerage, available 9am to 6pm Monday to Friday on 905-948-3599 or 1-855-622-2233, or directly to the insurer on that company's 24-hour toll-free claims line. _(public_company_claim)_
- **claims_carriers** — The claims page lists direct insurer claims lines for Chubb Insurance, Gore Mutual, Hagerty Canada and Pafco Insurance, with Pafco split between an Ontario and an Atlantic number. _(public_company_claim)_
- **compensation_model** — Ivantage states that it is compensated through commissions and fees paid by property and casualty insurers and by managing general agents for the sale of insurance products, and it publishes the commission percentage ranges it receives. _(public_company_claim)_
- **commission_ranges** — The published commission ranges are Pafco 10-12.5%, Pembridge 10-20%, Allstate 15-20%, Gore Mutual 10-20%, Hagerty Canada 15%, Northbridge 4-5%, April Canada 15-20%, Premier Canada Assurance Managers 15-25%, Risk-Can Underwriting Managers 15%, Special Risk Insurance Managers 15% and PAL Insurance Brokers $20-$40. _(public_company_claim)_
- **market_panel** — The commission disclosure names eleven insurer and MGA relationships. Alongside standard carriers it includes specialty markets: April Canada, Premier Canada Assurance Managers Ltd., Risk-Can Underwriting Managers, Special Risk Insurance Managers and PAL Insurance Brokers. _(public_company_claim)_
- **contingent_commission** — Ivantage discloses that it can periodically receive a contingent commission, that Pafco Insurance and Gore Mutual Insurance are the markets marked as recognising its efforts that way, that payment is not guaranteed, and that it depends on growth, profitability measured by loss ratio, volume, retention and services provided, assessed across the whole portfolio with that insurer rather than on individual policies. _(public_company_claim)_
- **privacy_joint** — Ivantage operates under a joint privacy policy with Allstate Insurance Company of Canada, Pembridge Insurance Company and PAFCO Insurance Company, covering applications, customer interactions, website use and mobile apps. _(public_company_claim)_
- **privacy_collection** — The privacy policy states that personal information is collected directly, from third parties including industry data banks, credit reporting agencies, property inspectors and fraud prevention agencies, and from government bodies and law enforcement for verification, including motor vehicle and driving history records. _(public_company_claim)_
- **call_recording** — The privacy policy states that calls, online chats and other communications may be recorded for quality, confirmation of instructions, complaint resolution and staff training, and that a customer who does not want communications recorded can do business by visiting an office or by writing or emailing instead. _(public_company_claim)_
- **telematics** — The privacy policy names Drivewise and My_Bridge as telematics programs, collecting geolocation and driving data such as vehicle speed, distance travelled, time of day, acceleration and deceleration in order to calculate the program discount. _(public_company_claim)_
- **accessibility_plan** — Ivantage publishes an Accessible Customer Service Plan under the Accessibility for Ontarians with Disabilities Act, 2005, covering assistive devices, alternative communication formats, service animals, support persons and posted notice of temporary service disruptions on its website. _(public_company_claim)_
- **staff_training** — The accessibility plan commits to training all employees and volunteers within three months of hire, covering the AODA customer service standard, the company plan, how to interact with people with various disabilities, assistive devices, service animals and support persons, and what to do when someone has difficulty accessing services. Employees are retrained when the plan changes. _(public_company_claim)_
- **complaint_loop** — Accessibility feedback and complaints route to the Ombudsman office at ombudsman@helpforyou.ca or Ombudsman, 27 Allstate Parkway, Suite 100, Markham, ON L3R 5P8, and the published commitment is that customers hear back within two business days. _(public_company_claim)_
- **consumer_rights** — The site links out to the Insurance Bureau of Canada Code of Consumer Rights and Responsibilities and to Allstate Canada's Public Accountability Statement rather than restating them. _(public_company_claim)_
- **site_scope** — The public site is a five-page brochure: Home, Claims, Disclosure of Commissions, Privacy Policy and Accessibility, plus outbound links to allstate.ca and the IBC. No online quoting, product catalogue, office locator, broker roster, careers page or pricing was published on the pages observed. _(public_company_claim)_

**Room exercises and measures**

- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: Write the one service promise you would publish on your own site, then name the hours, the fallback path and the response time you would be willing to print next to it.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Percentage of service contacts answered inside your own published response window.
- _The Operating System_ exercise: Pick the one thing your agency does that you would not currently be comfortable publishing, and decide whether the fix is the wording or the practice.
- _The Operating System_ measure: Number of core policies written down and dated versus still living in the owner's head.
- _Making It Rain_ exercise: List the risks you turned away last quarter, then name which market you would have needed on the panel to keep them.
- _Making It Rain_ measure: Quotes placed outside your primary market, as a share of total quotes.
- _Commitment to Training_ exercise: Set your own hire-to-trained deadline in days, name what must be covered before it expires, and decide who checks.
- _Commitment to Training_ measure: Days from hire to completed onboarding training, per new hire.
- _The Sales Sequence_ exercise: Rank your markets by what they actually pay you, then check whether your producers quote in that order.
- _The Sales Sequence_ measure: Mix of bound policies by market, against the commission each one pays.

**On these resource pages**

- **Operating System Resources** (/resources/operating-system)
  - Helps with: A working example of agency policy written down in public: compensation ranges, contingent commission, privacy and accessibility.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves moving core policy out of your head and into writing you would be comfortable publishing.

**Open questions**

- ENTITY IDENTITY — READ BEFORE RE-RESEARCHING. This record describes Ivantage Insurance Brokers Inc., the Canadian brokerage at ivantageinsurance.ca, confirmed by the product owner on 2026-09-09. A separate and unrelated US entity called Ivantage Select Agency, an Allstate affiliate, also exists and has no public website. Do not merge, cross-reference or re-conflate the two.
- Pricing. Nothing is priced on the site — a brokerage quotes per risk — so no pricing is recorded here. The only published rates are the commissions Ivantage receives from insurers, which are its revenue, not a customer price.
- Any outcome, retention, growth, premium or customer-count figure. The site publishes none and none were asserted.
- Head office and branch locations. The published phone numbers are 905 area code and the Ombudsman mailing address is in Markham, Ontario, but no Ivantage office address, locator or branch list was published on the pages observed.
- Named representative, leadership, broker roster and headcount. No individual is named anywhere on the site, so the onboarding contact is unresolved.
- Whether the eleven markets in the commission disclosure are the complete current panel, and when that table was last updated. The page carries no revision date.
- The exact Formula partnerOrgId, the authenticated representative UID and verified membership. No organization was bound and no representative account was verified.
- Ivantage's own org chart, assigned owners, capacity, budget, baseline KPIs and 2027 targets.

### 22. YPC Media

- **Tier:** Bronze
- **Website:** https://www.ypcmedia.com
- **Logo:** `/assets/sponsors/ypc-media.png`
- **Resource-page id:** `ypc-media`
- **Category:** Full-service online marketing agency — local SEO, listings, reputation, web, social and paid advertising for small businesses and insurance agents
- **Also known as:** YPC Media Online Marketing, YPC
- **Research version:** 2026-09-09.1, reviewed 2026-09-09

**What we know (23 researched facts)**

- **company_shape** — YPC Media describes itself as a full-service online marketing and sales organization specialising in helping small to medium sized businesses establish or grow their internet presence. It is based at 75 Town Centre Dr, Rochester, NY, reachable at sales@ypcmedia.com and 800-847-9760. _(public_company_claim)_
- **three_pillars** — The whole offering is organised into three pillars the client chooses between: Be Found (search visibility), Get Noticed (brand and reputation) and Generate Leads (paid advertising). _(public_company_claim)_
- **insurance_vertical** — Insurance is a named Premier Industry with its own page, split in the site navigation into Captive Insurance and Independent Insurance. The other premier industries listed are paintless dent repair, auto detailing, real estate, professional B2C and fencing. _(public_company_claim)_
- **insurance_promises** — The insurance page states six things an agent gets: exclusive leads, transparency, superior service, live reporting, Certified Google Partner status and 20+ years in business. _(public_company_claim)_
- **exclusive_definition** — The company defines exclusive leads explicitly: "you are the only business owner who is getting that lead. We do not resell or share your lead information." _(public_company_claim)_
- **account_manager** — Each client is assigned a designated Account Manager who reviews results on a monthly or quarterly cadence chosen by the client and is available for questions between reviews. _(public_company_claim)_
- **reporting** — Reporting is a live reporting link emailed to the client on the 1st of every month showing stats and campaign results in real time, walked through by the Account Manager on the agreed cadence. _(public_company_claim)_
- **no_contracts** — The company states there are no contracts, that monthly payment options are now offered, and that it will not hold a client hostage. _(public_company_claim)_
- **marketing_score** — Pre-sale diagnosis is a marketing score: a Business Development Executive assesses where the business currently stands online and recommends services from that. A free SEO audit is also offered from the homepage in exchange for a website URL, name and email. _(public_company_claim)_
- **process** — The delivery process is published as five steps: Listen (full marketing consultation and industry research), Identify (choose products and services), Implement (build and launch the campaign), Support (monitor the account with a dedicated support team) and Drive (results). _(public_company_claim)_
- **befound_services** — Be Found covers local search with consistent name, address and phone across the web, Google Business Profile optimization, a local search audit, business listing management syndicated to top directories, SEO keyword research, link building and on-site content, map search publication to Google, Apple and Bing Maps, and voice search optimization targeting Alexa, Siri, Cortana and Google Home. _(public_company_claim)_
- **getnoticed_services** — Get Noticed covers reputation management with custom written review responses and review generation tools, website design and hosting built for mobile and SEO, paid and organic audience growth, graphic design including logo creation, video production, and a product named Social Boost – Insurance. _(public_company_claim)_
- **leads_services** — Generate Leads covers search engine marketing across Google and Microsoft, pay-per-click campaign creation, optimization and management with keyword research and bidding strategy, social media advertising run by Facebook Blueprint certified specialists, and programmatic advertising across Google, Facebook, YouTube, streaming services and display networks. _(public_company_claim)_
- **market_exclusivity** — The pay-per-click offering states that market exclusivity is available for specific industries, meaning a territory can be locked to one client. _(public_company_claim)_
- **product_catalogue** — The navigation names individual purchasable products, including SpeedListings and SpeedListings+, Local Citation Wizard, GBP one-time optimization, WordPress websites, domain purchasing and hosting, Reputation Management+, Review Monitoring, Email Signatures, Tap Cards, Reviewzy, OS+, Custom Organic, Social Suite, Google Guarantee, Google Shopping Ads, Amazon Ads, call tracking, email hosting, live reporting and Zoho CRM support. _(public_company_claim)_
- **hourly_consulting** — Hourly Consulting is sold as a standalone line item alongside the managed services, so advisory time can be bought without a campaign. _(public_company_claim)_
- **core_values** — The published core values are dedicated, relentless, innovative, versatile and ethical. _(public_company_claim)_
- **resources** — The company publishes a blog and runs client webinars, which several reviewers cite as part of the service rather than an extra. _(public_company_claim)_
- **scale_claims** — Published scale claims include $6M in annual ad spend managed for clients, over 1M exclusive leads for customers last year, over 40,000 businesses helped, 20+ years in business, and a Rochester Top 100 company standing. Several counters render as blanks on the page and none are dated. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **review_volume** — The homepage displays an EXCELLENT rating based on 254 reviews through a third-party review widget. The figure was not independently checked. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **third_party_stats** — The site cites uncited third-party statistics — 88% of local business searchers calling or visiting within 24 hours, 72% of local searchers visiting a store within five miles, over 58% of consumers using voice search, an estimated 5.6 billion daily Google searches, 144 minutes a day on social and 5X longer attention on video than static posts. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **testimonial_outcomes** — Client testimonials assert business growth, live leads driven and marketing understanding gained. They are individual customer statements, undated and unverified. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **vertical_pages_missing** — The navigation offers Captive Insurance and Independent Insurance as separate destinations, but both /captive-insurance/ and /independent-insurance/ returned the site's not-found page when loaded. Only the single /insurance/ page exists. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_

**Room exercises and measures**

- _Making It Rain_ exercise: Sort your current spend into be found, get noticed and generate leads, then name the pillar you are underfunding.
- _Making It Rain_ measure: Leads and quoted households by pillar, against spend by pillar.
- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: Decide who in your office asks for a review, at what moment in the transaction, and what they say.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Reviews requested versus reviews received per month, and average rating trend.
- _The Operating System_ exercise: Name who inside your agency reads the 1st-of-month report before you do. If nobody does, that is the gap.
- _The Operating System_ measure: Monthly reports reviewed on time by an internal owner, out of twelve.
- _The Sales Sequence_ exercise: Take last month's tracked calls and decide the one follow-up standard every one of them gets.
- _The Sales Sequence_ measure: Tracked calls that reached a quote, by producer.

**On these resource pages**

- **Making It Rain Resources** (/resources/making-it-rain)
  - Helps with: Digital marketing for agencies — SEO, business listings, website design, reputation management and paid advertising.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves the marketing surface around your agency: site, listings, reviews and ads.

**Open questions**

- All pricing. No price is published for any product, bundle or the monthly payment option; everything is quoted after a marketing score conversation.
- What the agency has to supply. Website access, Google Business Profile ownership, ad account access, brand assets and approval turnaround are not described anywhere.
- Which industries qualify for market exclusivity on pay-per-click, and whether insurance is one of them.
- How leads are delivered to the agent — email, CRM push, phone routing — and what happens to a lead that is never worked.
- What Social Boost – Insurance actually includes and how it differs from standard social posting.
- Whether the client or YPC owns the website, domain, ad accounts and listing profiles after the relationship ends. No-contract terms make this material.
- Carrier and captive-brand compliance handling for ad copy, review responses and social posts.
- The difference between the captive and independent insurance offerings. Both navigation destinations return not-found pages.
- What the live reporting link actually reports — whether it shows bound premium or only impressions, clicks and calls.

### 23. National General

- **Tier:** Bronze
- **Website:** https://nationalgeneral.com
- **Logo:** `/assets/sponsors/national-general.png`
- **Resource-page id:** `national-general`
- **Category:** Personal and small-commercial property and casualty carrier distributing through independent agents
- **Also known as:** National General Insurance, NatGen, National General Group, National General, an Allstate company
- **Research version:** 2026-09-09.1, reviewed 2026-09-09

**Podcast interview**

- Guest: Monica & Dom
- Vimeo id: 1223165390
- Contact: Call Dom → tel:+12168023959
- Shown as: monica.jenkins1@allstate.com · I813022@allstate.com · 216-802-3959
- Note: Monica and Dom invited agents to reach out directly. Dom shared his Allstate email and phone number for follow-up.

**What we know (25 researched facts)**

- **identity** — National General is an Allstate company. It states it has been helping people insure what matters since 1920, with roots in the early days of the automobile, and that policies are underwritten by individual carriers in the National General Group, Winston-Salem, NC — each underwriting company solely responsible for its own products, rates and claims. _(public_company_claim)_
- **distribution** — National General sells both directly to consumers and through a nationwide network of independent agents, and describes that independent agent network as its strength. Its agent-facing pages are aimed at independent agents and brokers rather than captive agents. _(public_company_claim)_
- **ratings** — The company states it is rated A+ by AM Best and A+ by the Better Business Bureau, and notes on the page that ratings are subject to change and that financial performance of the listed companies is not guaranteed by Allstate Insurance Company or its affiliates. _(public_company_claim)_
- **product_lines** — Published personal lines are auto, motorcycle, RV, homeowners, condo, renters, private collections, flood and umbrella, plus commercial vehicle. Home and auto package options are offered through NatGen Premier and Encompass Insurance, both described as products of independent agents of the Allstate family. An individual health page is also linked. _(public_company_claim)_
- **auto_appetite** — The stated auto appetite is deliberately broad across driver quality: the site says it offers different types of auto insurance for different types of drivers and specifically addresses drivers with a few tickets as well as those with a spotless record. It also claims more than 80 years of experience insuring vehicles. _(public_company_claim)_
- **auto_coverages** — Published auto coverages: bodily injury liability, property damage liability, collision, comprehensive, medical payments, uninsured motorist, underinsured motorist, personal injury protection, accidental death and dismemberment, roadside assistance, and an Auto Protection Plan. Auto Protection Plans and certain roadside products are stated to be third-party membership programs, not insurance. _(public_company_claim)_
- **auto_benefits** — Named auto benefits: DynamicDrive telematics; an Emergency Expense Allowance covering up to $500 in food, lodging and travel after a covered claim; the MyNatGenPolicy self-service site; 24-hour claims reporting with the company stating most minor claims are handled by phone and paid within 48 hours; and lifetime repair guarantees on collision work done at its Gold Medal Repair Shops, stated as possibly unavailable in some states or programs. _(public_company_claim)_
- **dynamicdrive** — DynamicDrive is a telematics app that scores braking, speeding, phone handling and time of day, gives the driver individualized feedback, and makes users in certain states eligible for a policy discount. It is offered on both personal auto and commercial vehicle. _(public_company_claim)_
- **auto_discounts** — Published auto discounts include bundle with home, pay in full, low mileage, security device, multi-vehicle, safe driver and paperless. _(public_company_claim)_
- **home_coverages** — Homeowners features published: Guaranteed Replacement Cost and Extended Replacement Cost for total loss; Green Upgrade options providing up to $50,000 to rebuild with environmentally friendly materials; scheduled coverage options for items such as watercraft, trailers, jewelry, watches and furs; Identity Theft 911 as a free restoration service; and Identity Theft Expense Reimbursement paying up to $15,000 for certain restoration expenses. Condo and renters are written alongside. _(public_company_claim)_
- **home_discounts** — Published homeowners discounts and credits: Age of Home, Auto Companion, Auto/Home Account, Claim Free, College Education, Gated Community, Home Buyer, Homeowners Association, Insurance to Value, Prime Time, Protective Devices and Superior Construction. _(public_company_claim)_
- **flood_two_paths** — National General offers two flood paths: Beyond Floods, its private product, and an NFIP policy. Beyond Floods is published as covering a single-family structure up to $1.5 million against NFIP's $250,000, with a 7-day waiting period against NFIP's 30 days (both 0 days at lender closing). _(public_company_claim)_
- **flood_terms** — Further published Beyond Floods differences against NFIP: replacement cost loss settlement on dwelling and available on contents rather than actual cash value; basement building and contents coverage available where NFIP offers none; loss of use up to $150,000 where NFIP offers none; annual, semi-annual, quarterly or mortgagee billing rather than annual only; and flat fully earned fees regardless of occupancy. _(public_company_claim)_
- **flood_availability** — Beyond Floods is underwritten by companies in the National General Insurance Group, NatGen Flood Division. The page states it is admitted in New Jersey and Florida and written as excess and surplus lines in a listed set of 30 other states — a list that includes Indiana. _(public_company_claim)_
- **flood_education** — The flood page argues appetite through risk education: everyone lives in a flood zone, floods and flash floods occur in all 50 states, 25% of all flood claims are filed by people outside high-risk areas, and in high-risk areas a home is twice as likely to be damaged by flood than by fire. _(public_company_claim)_
- **commercial_auto** — Commercial Vehicle is aimed at small- and mid-sized businesses with a vehicle fleet. Published coverages include Tools Coverage, Non-Owned Auto, Any Auto, Contractual Liability for Additional Insureds, Pollution Liability and Accidental Death and Dismemberment. Published discounts include Multi-Policy, In-Agency Transfer, Paid-in-Full, AutoPay, DynamicDrive and Trade Associations. _(public_company_claim)_
- **agent_value_prop** — The stated case to agents has four parts: financial stability as an Allstate company; a wide range of products on the argument that multi-policy customers stay with their agent longer; highly competitive commissions on both initial and repeat business with bonuses unlocked for selling multiple policies to the same customer; and a technology-first posture with a policy management platform built specifically for independent agents. _(public_company_claim)_
- **agent_services** — Agent- and customer-facing services published: 24/7 claim reporting, live claims chat for both agents and customers, eSignature, catastrophe response with stated boots on the ground during major events, and paperless billing. _(public_company_claim)_
- **appointment_path** — Appointment runs through a published Get Appointed application described as a five-step process, after which the company states it will be in touch. _(public_company_claim)_
- **independent_agent_stance** — The company states plainly that independent agents and brokers are not its employees, represent several carriers, and work for the customer rather than the carrier — including shopping quotes across companies and advocating for the customer on claims. It is not a captive distribution posture. _(public_company_claim)_
- **state_variation** — Every product page carries the same material caveat: all coverages, discounts, features, benefits and pay plans vary by state and by insurer, and are subject to availability, eligibility, terms, conditions and exclusions. Nothing listed can be assumed available in a given state without checking. _(public_company_claim)_
- **agent_count** — National General publishes a network of more than 55,000 independent agents nationwide. The figure is undated and appears on both the homepage and Our Story with no as-of date. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **size_claim** — The company describes itself as one of the largest insurers in the United States, with no rank, premium volume, source or date given. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **history_inconsistency** — Two different histories are published: Our Story says the company has been helping people insure since 1920, while the auto page claims more than 80 years of experience insuring vehicles. The two do not reconcile and neither was independently verified. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **claim_speed_claim** — The claim that most minor auto claims are handled over the phone and paid within 48 hours is an undated vendor service claim with no measurement, sample or exception stated. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_

**Sources**

- National General, an Allstate company — Home — https://nationalgeneral.com/ (seen 2026-09-09)
- Our Story — National General Insurance — https://nationalgeneral.com/about-us/our-story (seen 2026-09-09)
- Working with National General (agents and brokers) — https://nationalgeneral.com/agents/working-with-us (seen 2026-09-09)

**Workbook fit**

| Session | Fit | Where it lands | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The Sales Sequence | primary | Position Definition & Focus % | Carrier appetite sets the outer edge of what a producer can actually sell, and a market that writes drivers with tickets alongside clean records changes who a producer is allowed to work rather than decline. Multi-policy commission bonuses make bundling a compensation decision, not just a coaching one. The call itself, and the standard you hold producers to, stay yours. |
| Making It Rain | supporting_direct | Your Marketing Strategy | Appetite is a marketing input before it is an underwriting one: private flood at $1.5 million with a 7-day wait, RV, motorcycle, collections and small-fleet commercial each define a niche an agency could deliberately go after. The carrier supplies the market, not the campaign, the spend or the leads. |
| Growth Through Service | supporting_direct | Synchronicity | The carrier's own service rails — 24/7 claim reporting, live claims chat for agents, eSignature, paperless billing, catastrophe response — determine how much of a service moment the agency has to carry itself. The multi-policy retention argument is a service argument in commission clothing. What the client experiences from your office is still your design. |
| The Operating System | conditional | Your Agency Bible | Conditional, and the condition is the caveat printed on every product page: coverages, discounts and pay plans vary by state and insurer. That is exactly the kind of rule that must live in writing rather than in one veteran's head — which market, which risk, which state, which discount. The carrier supplies no operating documentation of its own. |
| Commitment to Training | not_recommended | — | No training curriculum, certification, CE program or agent education offering was published on the pages observed. Product and appetite documentation is not training, and treating it as such would overstate what the carrier provides. |

**Room exercises and measures**

- _The Sales Sequence_ exercise: Pull last month's declines and mark which ones this appetite would have let you quote instead of walk away from.
- _The Sales Sequence_ measure: Quote-to-bind rate on risks previously declined, and items per household on new business.
- _Making It Rain_ exercise: Pick one niche this appetite opens, name ten specific prospects in it, and decide who owns the outreach.
- _Making It Rain_ measure: New business premium from the chosen niche, tracked separately from general new business.
- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: Walk one claim from first notice to payment and mark every step your team performs that the carrier already offers a rail for.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Items per household and multi-line retention, tracked against single-line accounts.
- _The Operating System_ exercise: Write the one-page placement rule: which risks go to this market first, and which never do.
- _The Operating System_ measure: Whether the placement rule exists in writing and new staff can apply it without asking.

**On these resource pages**

- **Operating System Resources** (/resources/operating-system)
  - Helps with: Market placement rules for a carrier whose coverages, discounts and pay plans vary by state.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves writing down which risks go to which market, instead of asking the one veteran who knows.

**Open questions**

- Commission schedules and bonus structure. 'Highly competitive commissions' and multi-policy bonuses are claimed, but no rate, tier or qualification threshold is published anywhere on the public site.
- Appointment requirements: production minimums, E&O limits, licensing, state availability of appointments, and how long the five-step application takes to decide.
- State-by-state availability of every product, coverage and discount listed. The site's own caveat is that all of it varies by state and insurer.
- Underwriting guidelines and true appetite detail — acceptable loss history, prior-carrier requirements, credit use, roof age, coastal or wildfire restrictions. Nothing beyond marketing-level appetite is public.
- Which named underwriting companies within the National General Group write which line in a given state; the site attributes products to 'individual carriers in the National General Group'.
- The independent agent policy management platform is described but never named, demoed or documented. Rating, download, AMS integration and service capability are all unknown.
- Whether Beyond Floods excess and surplus placement in a given state requires a surplus lines licence and what filing burden that puts on the agency.
- How NatGen Premier and Encompass relate to a National General appointment — whether they are separate appointments, separate commission schedules or separate systems.
- Any agent-facing performance data: quote turnaround, hit ratios, service levels, claim cycle times or agent satisfaction.
- Whether any agency support, marketing co-op, or field representative coverage exists in Indiana.
- The agent login, lender services and routing tools linked in the navigation were not opened; they are gated or out of scope for public research.

**Activation notes**

- runtimeSurface: Partner-seat business workbooks only (s1, s2, s4, s5, s7)
- identityStatus: no_partner_organization_bound
- disclosure: Research compiled from National General's own public website only. It is a large Allstate-owned carrier, not a Formula vendor partner; nothing here was supplied or reviewed by the company, and no claim was independently verified.
- note: Approve the record, bind the exact existing organization, then separately authorize the Firebase Functions release. Book mapping is editorial analysis, not partner-approved placement.

### 24. DMS

- **Tier:** Bronze
- **Website:** https://digitalmediasolutions.com
- **Logo:** `/assets/sponsors/dms.png`
- **Resource-page id:** `dms`
- **Category:** Performance marketing and consumer acquisition; insurance leads, calls and clicks, with ZipQuote as its agent-facing lead platform
- **Also known as:** Digital Media Solutions, Digital Media Solutions, LLC, DMS Group, ZipQuote, Sparkroom
- **Research version:** 2026-09-09.1, reviewed 2026-09-09

**What we know (22 researched facts)**

- **identity** — DMS is Digital Media Solutions, LLC, a performance marketing company that connects consumers and brands through data, technology and its own media platforms. Its published core verticals are auto and home insurance, health insurance, home services, education and consumer finance. _(public_company_claim)_
- **audience_is_brands_not_agents** — DMS itself sells to advertisers, carriers and enterprise agency pools rather than to individual agencies. Its published agent-level entry point is ZipQuote, which DMS describes as its self-service platform for agents with CRM integration, real-time performance insights and campaign support. _(public_company_claim)_
- **three_products** — DMS publishes three customer acquisition products. Clicks are exclusive linkout campaigns that send traffic to the advertiser's own landing page, so conversion happens on the advertiser's site. Calls use DMS call center representatives to pre-qualify a consumer and transfer them live to the buyer's sales team. Digital Inquiries are leads captured through a multi-step process that qualifies the prospect and secures express written consent. _(public_company_claim)_
- **pricing_model** — DMS describes its model as results-based pricing, stating it only charges when results are delivered. No rate, minimum or fee schedule is published. _(public_company_claim)_
- **media_sourcing** — Traffic comes from a mix of DMS owned-and-operated websites, targeted media buys and a vetted publisher network. Named owned-and-operated health properties are Medicare Deluxe, Health Market Advisor and Protect Health. _(public_company_claim)_
- **agent_controls_dms** — For auto and home, DMS publishes three buyer-side capabilities: custom audience building with granular filters for buying control, real-time inquiry verification with consumer qualification scoring, and campaign reporting with automatic analysis of returns. _(public_company_claim)_
- **compliance_posture** — DMS states it operates in-house legal and compliance teams, uses lead verification and consent-tracking technology, and treats compliance as central in regulated verticals such as health insurance and education. Its General Counsel is also titled EVP of Compliance. _(public_company_claim)_
- **leadership** — Published DMS leadership: Tim Robinson (CEO), Rick Fox (Chief Growth Officer), Jen Robinson (Chief Technology Officer), Brian Hirschfeld (Chief Financial Officer) and Tony Saldana (General Counsel and EVP of Compliance). _(public_company_claim)_
- **zipquote_identity** — ZipQuote is the agent-facing lead platform in the DMS family, stating it has operated since 2008. It sells internet leads and live call transfers for auto and home insurance, describing its leads as internally generated, non-incentivized and validated. _(public_company_claim)_
- **zipquote_lines** — ZipQuote's published lead types are auto and home, with live transfer as a delivery form. Its sign-up form also lists health as a lead-type option, but the FAQ states the platform offers high-intent leads for home and auto. _(public_company_claim)_
- **zipquote_calls** — ZipQuote live call transfers are pre-qualified by a concierge team that engages the consumer, confirms interest, and transfers the call to the agent's phone in real time, with the agent described as first on the line. Calls are sold as exclusive. _(public_company_claim)_
- **zipquote_controls** — ZipQuote states an agent can personalise lead delivery, controlling the timing and frequency of leads to match the business model, and that lead caps exist so the agent is not paying for volume beyond what was wanted. Campaign optimisation and performance monitoring are self-service. _(public_company_claim)_
- **zipquote_returns** — ZipQuote return requests must be submitted within 7 calendar days of delivery, and pending returns are processed weekly. Published credit reasons are invalid phone, consumer does not exist, consumer did not request a quote, language barrier, a duplicate from the past 30 days, and a lead that does not meet the agent's filter requirements. _(public_company_claim)_
- **zipquote_pricing** — ZipQuote publishes no price. Pricing is described as competitive and based on lead volume and requirements, quoted by the sales team. A $50 offer for new sign-ups is advertised, which is a promotional credit and not a price. _(public_company_claim)_
- **zipquote_integrations** — ZipQuote states it integrates with a range of lead management, sales automation and insurance tools so leads can be routed into the agent's own systems. No named integration list was published on the pages observed. _(public_company_claim)_
- **zipquote_support** — ZipQuote publishes dedicated support by live chat, email and phone during business hours, plus help with campaign management and performance analysis from expert advisors. _(public_company_claim)_
- **other_platforms** — DMS also operates separate logins for Sparkroom and DMS Direct and runs a publisher-side affiliate program, so an agency dealing with DMS may encounter more than one brand and portal. _(public_company_claim)_
- **reach_claims_unverified** — DMS states its internal media reaches over a hundred million adult consumers every month, drives hundreds of thousands of inquiry, click and call conversions across its owned-and-operated portfolio, and that it processes more than 60 billion signals monthly. These are undated vendor scale claims and were not verified. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **zipquote_stats_unverified** — ZipQuote advertises 14% higher written premium, 28% higher quote rate, 76% higher policy rate and 42% lower cost per policy, footnoted only as based on proprietary statistics or proprietary customer data. No baseline, comparison set or period is stated, so none of it is confirmable. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **contact_rate_claim_unverified** — ZipQuote claims a 100% contact rate on live call transfers on the grounds that consumers are pre-qualified before transfer, and states clients are achieving closing ratios up to 11% on those calls. Both are vendor outcome claims and were not verified. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **case_studies_unverified** — DMS publishes case studies claiming a 1.6x increase in policy rates from weekend campaigns, a 10x increase in lead volume from an AI-driven email campaign, and 16x year-over-year growth for a Medicare client. The clients are unnamed and the figures are vendor-reported. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **brand_trust_claims_unverified** — Both sites carry unnamed 'trusted by leading brands' logo walls and self-descriptions such as industry-leading compliance and premier lead vendor. No customer is identified in text on the pages observed. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_

**Room exercises and measures**

- _Making It Rain_ exercise: Say out loud which of the three your team is actually staffed to work today: a click that lands on your own page, a live call, or an emailed inquiry. Then check what you are currently buying.
- _Making It Rain_ measure: Cost per bound policy split by product type, not blended.
- _The Sales Sequence_ exercise: Set the hours you will accept live transfers, then name who answers and what happens when they do not.
- _The Sales Sequence_ measure: Transferred calls answered on the first attempt, by producer.
- _The Operating System_ exercise: Write who checks returnable leads, on which day of the week, and against which six credit reasons.
- _The Operating System_ measure: Returns filed inside the seven-day window as a percentage of leads that qualified.
- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: Trace one live-transferred consumer from answered call to bound policy and mark where the record is typed in by hand.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Manual re-entry steps between transfer and a quote in your rater.

**On these resource pages**

- **Making It Rain Resources** (/resources/making-it-rain)
  - Helps with: Performance marketing and consumer acquisition, including insurance leads, calls and clicks.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves sourcing opportunity through a media partner instead of building the funnel yourself.

**Open questions**

- All pricing. DMS describes results-based pricing and ZipQuote describes volume-based pricing, but no rate, minimum spend or deposit is published on either site.
- How many agents a ZipQuote lead is sold to. Live call transfers are marketed as exclusive; web lead distribution is not stated anywhere on the pages observed.
- The ZipQuote filter catalogue. Filters are referenced in the return policy as the agent's own setting, but no list of available filters is published.
- The ZipQuote CRM and rater integration list. Integration is claimed generically with no named systems.
- Whether ZipQuote sells health leads. The sign-up form lists health as a lead type while the FAQ describes home and auto only.
- The corporate relationship and current ownership structure between Digital Media Solutions, LLC and ZipQuote. The DMS site names ZipQuote as its agent solution and links its login, but no ownership statement was published on the pages observed.
- Contract length, minimum commitment and cancellation terms for a ZipQuote account.
- Any independent verification of the ZipQuote performance percentages or the DMS case study figures. All are vendor-published and recorded here as unconfirmed.

- runtimeSurface: Partner-seat business workbooks only (s1, s2, s4, s5, s7)

### 25. LeadMiner

- **Tier:** Bronze
- **Website:** https://leadminer.ai
- **Logo:** `/assets/sponsors/leadminer.png`
- **Resource-page id:** `leadminer`
- **Category:** Outsourced lead follow-up and warm-transfer contact center for insurance agencies
- **Also known as:** LeadMiner, Lead Miner AI, leadminer.ai
- **Research version:** 2026-09-09.1, reviewed 2026-09-09

**Podcast interview**

- Guest: Scott
- Vimeo id: 1223169978
- Contact: Book with Scott → https://leadminer.ai
- Shown as: scott@leadminer.ai · leadminer.ai
- Note: Scott invited agents to email him directly or book a call through LeadMiner's website.

**What we know (16 researched facts)**

- **what_it_is** — Lead Miner describes itself as a provider of lead generation and customer service solutions for insurance agencies, whose teams contact and nurture an agency's new leads and turn them into warm transfers for the agency's licensed agents. Its published tagline is 'Stop dialing and start quoting'. _(public_company_claim)_
- **human_teams** — The service is delivered by people, not software the agency operates. Lead Miner calls them 'Superhuman teams' and describes itself as a contact center performing outreach on the agency's behalf, positioned as an extension of the agency. _(public_company_claim)_
- **leadership_background** — The company states that over the past 20 years its leadership team has built, operated and contracted with contact centers across the globe and has employed thousands of support and sales agents. It states its specialty is lead nurture, follow-up and conversion rather than lead origination. _(public_company_claim)_
- **coverage_hours** — Lead Miner states its system works days, nights and weekends, framing extended-hours coverage as the mechanism for maximising return on lead spend. _(public_company_claim)_
- **four_promises** — The four stated benefits are: higher return on lead spend through the highest contact rate; focus, so the sales team works active buyers instead of chasing leads; improved sales-agent morale by removing the telemarketing grind; and a white-glove approach whose first priority is protecting the agency's brand with prospects. _(public_company_claim)_
- **core_thesis** — Lead Miner's stated thesis is that speed to lead and consistent follow-up are the keys to maximising conversion, and that both are what in-house sales teams fail to sustain. _(public_company_claim)_
- **engagement_steps** — The published engagement sequence is: understand the agency's current process and consult on results; integrate with the agency's lead sources; perform consistent outreach and convert leads into warm transfers; then provide reporting back to the agency. _(public_company_claim)_
- **lead_source_integration** — Lead Miner states it integrates with the agency's existing lead sources so it can work leads the agency is already buying, rather than supplying its own leads. _(public_company_claim)_
- **reporting** — The company states it provides actionable reporting and will help the agency determine whether it is purchasing the right number of leads. _(public_company_claim)_
- **warm_transfer_handoff** — The handoff model is a live warm transfer: Lead Miner's team qualifies the lead and transfers a prepared prospect to the agency's licensed agent, who does the quoting and closing. Lead Miner does not describe quoting or binding on the agency's behalf. _(public_company_claim)_
- **tcpa_commitment** — Lead Miner publishes a TCPA compliance commitment covering five practices: obtaining explicit consent before outbound calls, updating do-not-call lists to avoid contacting opt-outs, limiting calling hours, having agents identify themselves and the company they represent at the start of each call, and promptly honouring do-not-call requests. _(public_company_claim)_
- **security_posture** — The company states it uses encryption, access controls and regular security audits to protect consumer information. No certification, auditor or standard is named on the page observed. _(public_company_claim)_
- **contact_route** — The only published route in is a scheduling form requiring name, email, phone and message, plus the address info@leadminer.ai. The call to action is a free consultation. _(public_company_claim)_
- **compliant_contact_center** — Lead Miner describes itself as a highly compliant contact center adhering to the strictest industry standards. No specific standard, licence or certification is named on the page observed. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **site_depth** — The entire public site is a single page: the nav items About Us, How it Works, TCPA and Contact Us are anchors on the homepage. The published sitemap lists only that homepage plus an unmodified WordPress sample page, so no product, pricing, team, case-study or client-reference content exists to verify. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **no_ai_detail** — Despite the .ai domain, no page observed describes any AI, automation or software product. Every capability described is human contact-center outreach. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_

**Sources**

- Lead Miner — single-page site (Home, About Us, How it Works, TCPA, Contact Us) — https://leadminer.ai/ (seen 2026-09-09)
- Lead Miner — published XML sitemap — https://leadminer.ai/wp-sitemap-posts-page-1.xml (seen 2026-09-09)

| Making It Rain | primary | The Opportunity Distribution | Lead Miner takes over what happens to a purchased lead between arrival and the producer's first real conversation, and reports back on whether the lead volume is right. That is squarely the distribution of opportunity. It does not originate leads or set the marketing strategy. |
| The Sales Sequence | supporting_direct | Position Definition & Focus % | Removing chase work from the producer seat is a position-definition decision before it is a vendor decision: if outreach leaves the seat, the focus percentage and the accountability standard both have to be rewritten around live conversations. The producer's own call stays with the agency. |
| Growth Through Service | conditional | Synchronicity | Conditional, and the condition is unresolved: the company names customer service in its own description but every capability on the page is new-lead outreach. An outside team speaking to prospects under the agency's brand is a service-standard question regardless, since the prospect experiences them as the agency. |

That written standard is the session's artifact. The vendor supplies no operating documentation of its own. |

**Room exercises and measures**

- _Making It Rain_ exercise: Count last month's leads, then count how many were dialed more than four times. The gap is what you are outsourcing.
- _Making It Rain_ measure: Contact rate and warm transfers delivered per hundred leads, by source.
- _The Sales Sequence_ exercise: Rewrite your producer's job description assuming they never dial a cold lead again, then name what you would hold them to instead.
- _The Sales Sequence_ measure: Producer hours spent in live qualified conversations versus outbound dialing.
- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: Write the first sixty seconds of the call exactly as you want a stranger to deliver it under your name.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Nothing yet — confirm what service work, if any, is actually in scope before setting one.
- _The Operating System_ exercise: Write the qualification bar in one sentence: what must be true before a lead reaches your agent?
- _The Operating System_ measure: Whether the outreach standard and transfer criteria exist in writing and are being followed.

**On these resource pages**

- **Making It Rain Resources** (/resources/making-it-rain)
  - Helps with: Lead nurture, qualification and moving engaged opportunities toward licensed agents.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves contact rates and getting purchased opportunity into live conversations.
- **Formula Sales Sequence Resources** (/resources/sales-sequence)
  - Helps with: Lead nurture, qualification and moving engaged opportunities toward licensed agents.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves improving contact rates, nurture or getting producers into more qualified conversations.

**Open questions**

- This record is thinner than the others by necessity. The whole public site is one page with no product, pricing, team, client or case-study content, so there is materially less to verify. Treat gaps as unresearched, not as absent capability.
- Pricing and commercial model entirely. No price, minimum, per-transfer rate or contract term is published; the only route is a free consultation.
- Company location, ownership, founding date and leadership names. The 20-year leadership claim names no person.
- Where the contact-center agents are located and whether they are employees or contracted, despite the stated global contact-center background.
- Whether the agents are licensed, and in which states, or whether all licensed activity stays with the agency after transfer.
- Which lead sources, CRMs or AMS platforms Lead Miner actually integrates with, and what the integration requires from the agency.
- Hours of operation in concrete terms. 'Days, nights and weekends' is stated without a schedule.
- What the reporting contains, how often it arrives, and whether the agency sees call recordings or transcripts.
- Any client reference, case study, volume figure or conversion result. None is published.
- Who holds TCPA consent for a given lead, how consent is evidenced, and how DNC scrubbing is performed and proven.
- What the .ai domain refers to. No AI or software capability is described anywhere on the site.
- Onboarding timeline and what the agency must supply to start.

**Activation notes**

### 26. ServiceMaster Restore

- **Tier:** Bronze
- **Website:** https://www.servicemasterrestore.com
- **Logo:** `/assets/sponsors/servicemaster-restore.svg`
- **Resource-page id:** `servicemaster-restore`
- **Category:** Franchised disaster restoration and mitigation network serving insurance carriers, agents and policyholders
- **Also known as:** ServiceMaster Restoration, ServiceMaster Restore USA, A ServiceMaster Company, servicemasterrestore.com
- **Research version:** 2026-09-09.1, reviewed 2026-09-09

**What we know (23 researched facts)**

- **what_it_is** — ServiceMaster Restore is a disaster restoration and mitigation provider for residential and commercial property, covering water damage, fire and smoke damage, mold remediation, odor damage, storm and weather damage, specialty services including board-up and packout, and reconstruction. It states 24/7/365 availability. _(public_company_claim)_
- **history** — The company states ServiceMaster was founded in 1929 by Marion E. Wade, a former minor league baseball player, and that ServiceMaster Restore has over 65 years of cleaning and restoration experience. _(public_company_claim)_
- **franchise_model** — Services are delivered by independently owned and operated franchises or by a corporate-owned branch, which the company frames as local ownership backed by a national network. This means terms, pricing and service quality are set locally, not centrally. _(public_company_claim)_
- **agent_audience** — Insurance agents are a named audience on the site alongside commercial, residential and national accounts, with a dedicated Insurance section built for agents referring their own policyholders. _(public_company_claim)_
- **ce_program** — ServiceMaster Restore publishes Continuing Education courses for insurance professionals and their staff, positioned around the claim that understanding mitigation lets an agent guide a policyholder through the right first steps and control claim costs. _(public_company_claim)_
- **ce_catalog** — Named CE courses include Water Damage Mitigation; Subrogation reporting on mitigation claims; Mold Remediation in Homes; Mold remediation removal and containment; Mitigating a Smoke and Odor Loss; Odor mitigation; Mitigating personal property packout claims; Reducing severities through an understanding of furniture damage and restoration; and an ethics course. _(public_company_claim)_
- **ce_content** — Course content is described concretely — for water, how long before damage occurs, the extraction process, drying and dehumidification principles, antimicrobials and odor control; for mold, growth and spread, containment, negative air and PPE; for smoke, how odor travels through a home in the seconds, minutes, hours and days after a loss. _(public_company_claim)_
- **mastermoments** — MasterMoments is a published three-step communication standard: walk the policyholder through the Emergency Recovery Guides to set expectations, call the agent within 24 hours with the policyholder's situation and claim status, and call the claim representative within 24 hours with the scope of damage, emergency services provided and claim information. _(public_company_claim)_
- **response_commitment** — For referred residential policyholders the company states it will arrive on site within four hours or less to begin mitigation, and that its emergency call center answers 24 hours a day, 7 days a week, 365 days a year at 866.867.3123. _(public_company_claim)_
- **three_steps** — The published recovery process is three steps: assessment (inspection and damage estimate to set scope), mitigation (preventing further damage to property and belongings), and restoration (returning the property to pre-loss condition). _(public_company_claim)_
- **claims_technology** — The company states it offers real-time mitigation claims management in fully mobile environments, on a cloud-based system that pushes updates to carriers and policyholders as a claim proceeds, integrates with key estimating software, and meets industry and data security standards. _(public_company_claim)_
- **commercial_scope** — Commercial capability is stated as reconstruction, content restoration, mobilization, building and site stabilization, pre-loss planning and large loss claim handling, plus ancillary site support such as portable lighting, restrooms, power, HVAC, fencing and dumpsters. _(public_company_claim)_
- **pre_loss_planning** — Pre-Loss Planning is offered to commercial clients as a proactive service covering risk assessment, response coordination and documentation before an event, intended to speed recovery afterwards. A Priority Response Program is also named. _(public_company_claim)_
- **carrier_agreements** — The company states it has agreements with some of the largest property insurers in the United States and runs a claims management department intended to smooth the process for both the homeowner and the insurance partner. _(public_company_claim)_
- **qrv_network** — The Quality Restoration Vendor (QRV) Network, established in 1991, is an internal standards program the company monitors franchise members against, covering accessibility, response time, communications capability and quality of results. The company states these standards are now referenced in industry Service Level Agreements and KPIs. _(public_company_claim)_
- **coverage_education** — The site publishes plain-language coverage education an agent can reuse with policyholders: sudden and accidental water discharge, burst pipes, water damage after a fire, roof-leak and storm-driven water, and ice dams are described as commonly covered, while flood, gradual or maintenance-related damage, intentional acts, earth movement, sewer backup and pool leaks are described as commonly not covered. _(public_company_claim)_
- **water_claim_frequency** — The policyholder page cites the Insurance Information Institute for the figures that about one in 50 homeowners files a water damage or freezing claim each year and that such claims account for 29% of all homeowners insurance claims. This is a cited third-party industry statistic, not a ServiceMaster performance figure. _(public_company_claim)_
- **local_dispatch** — The site routes work through a Find a Location search to the local franchise, and states franchise team members live in the communities they serve. Separate ServiceMaster Restore sites exist for the United Kingdom and Canada. _(public_company_claim)_
- **agent_resources** — The Insurance section splits into residential benefits, commercial benefits, policyholder information and CE classes, and the site publishes an ongoing blog aimed at facility managers and insurance professionals covering seasonal and catastrophe topics. _(public_company_claim)_
- **network_scale** — The About page claims more than 800 locations across the United States, additional locations in 14 countries, over $1 billion in annual customer revenue, and that the ServiceMaster family of brands served 5 million customers in the past year. All are undated marketing counts. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **satisfaction_claim** — The company claims one of the highest customer satisfaction scores in the industry, with no score, measurement method, source or period stated. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **reviews** — Homepage testimonials are five-star reviews attributed to first names only (Dennis, Rick, Chris & Kristine R.), undated and tied to no named location. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_
- **ce_logistics_unstated** — The CE page lists course topics but does not publish state approvals, credit hours, delivery format, schedule, cost or how an agency books a class. Whether any listed course carries CE credit in Indiana was not established. _(needs_confirmation, not used in AI)_

**Sources**

- Restoration Services | ServiceMaster Restore — https://servicemasterrestore.com/ (seen 2026-09-09)
- About ServiceMaster Restoration — https://servicemasterrestore.com/why-us/about-us (seen 2026-09-09)
- Insurance Continuing Education (CE) Courses for Insurance Professionals — https://servicemasterrestore.com/insurance/continuing-education-classes (seen 2026-09-09)
- Benefits & Services for Your Residential Insurance Policyholders — https://servicemasterrestore.com/insurance/residential-benefits-and-services (seen 2026-09-09)
- Benefits & Services for Your Commercial Insurance Policyholders — https://servicemasterrestore.com/insurance/commercial-benefits-and-services (seen 2026-09-09)
- Homeowners Insurance, Renters Insurance, and Water Damage Coverage — https://servicemasterrestore.com/insurance/policyholder-information (seen 2026-09-09)

**Workbook fit**

| Session | Fit | Where it lands | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Growth Through Service | primary | Synchronicity | A claim is the loudest service moment an agency has, and the published standard here is specifically about coordination — on site fast, agent called within 24 hours, adjuster called within 24 hours, updates flowing to everyone. Choosing and instructing a restoration partner is a service-design decision the agency owns; the vendor supplies the response, not the standard. |
| Commitment to Training | primary | The GOATs Train Every Day | This is a published CE curriculum aimed at the agency's licensed staff, on the exact subject matter they are worst at explaining: what mitigation is, what is covered, and what the policyholder must do in the first hours. It is outside content brought in, so the agency still decides who attends, what they must retain, and how that gets checked. |
| The Operating System | supporting_direct | Your Agency Bible | A named restoration partner, a phone number and a written first-notice procedure are exactly the kind of thing that should live in the agency bible instead of in whoever answered the phone. Because service is delivered by independently owned franchises, the local operator has to be named and vetted specifically, which is itself a documented decision. |
| Making It Rain | conditional | Your Marketing Strategy | Conditional, and the condition is strict: nothing published here generates a single lead for an agency. The only marketing-adjacent use is relationship-shaped — hosting a CE class or a commercial pre-loss planning conversation puts the agency in front of professionals and business owners. Treat that as a channel to build, not a capability to buy. |
| The Sales Sequence | not_recommended | — | No sales, quoting, lead-handling or producer capability was published on the pages observed. This is a claims-time vendor, and placing it in the sales session would misrepresent what it does. |
| The Body Session | not_recommended | — | Company research is excluded from the personal workbooks at runtime. |
| The Being Session | not_recommended | — | Company research is excluded from the personal workbooks at runtime. |
| The Balance Session | not_recommended | — | Company research is excluded from the personal workbooks at runtime. |
| My 2027 Map | reference_only | — | The Map compiles decisions already made in the other sessions; company research is not loaded here and should not introduce anything new. |

**Room exercises and measures**

- _Growth Through Service_ exercise: Walk one real water loss end to end and mark every hour nobody in your office knew what was happening.
- _Growth Through Service_ measure: Hours from first notice to on-site response, and whether the agent was called within 24 hours on each referred loss.
- _Commitment to Training_ exercise: Ask your service team, cold, which types of water damage are covered and which are not. Count the wrong answers.
- _Commitment to Training_ measure: Staff completing a mitigation or coverage course per year, and correct first-call guidance given on water losses.
- _The Operating System_ exercise: Write the loss-notification procedure on one page: who is called, in what order, and what the client is told first.
- _The Operating System_ measure: Whether the first-notice procedure exists in writing and was followed on the last five losses.
- _Making It Rain_ exercise: Name the ten commercial accounts you want and decide which would attend a pre-loss planning walkthrough.
- _Making It Rain_ measure: Nothing yet — confirm CE class availability and format before setting one.

**On these resource pages**

- **Growth Through Service Resources** (/resources/growth-through-service)
  - Helps with: Property restoration and mitigation support for customers after a loss.
  - Best fit: Your Domino involves the customer experience during a claim, when the relationship is most exposed.

**Open questions**

- No pricing of any kind is published, which is expected for restoration work — scope is priced per loss and, because franchises are independently owned, rates and terms are set locally.
- CE course logistics: state approvals, credit hours, delivery format, schedule, cost and how an agency books a class are all unpublished. Indiana approval specifically was not established.
- Which local franchise serves a given agency's territory, who owns it, and whether that operator meets the QRV standards the national site describes.
- Which property insurers the company holds agreements with. The claim names none.
- Whether an agent referral creates any obligation, compensation or preferred-vendor relationship, and whether that would raise a disclosure issue.
- Which estimating software the claims system integrates with, and whether an agent gets read access to claim status or only the 24-hour phone call.
- What the four-hour response commitment covers — all territories, all loss types, catastrophe conditions — and whether it is contractual or aspirational.
- The 'meets industry and data security standards' claim names no standard, certification or auditor.
- Whether ancillary services, reconstruction and large-loss capability are available from every franchise or only from selected locations.
- Any measured performance data: cycle times, reopen rates, complaint rates or carrier scorecard results.

**Activation notes**

- runtimeSurface: Partner-seat business workbooks only (s1, s2, s4, s5, s7)
- identityStatus: no_partner_organization_bound
